@\home\chu25\colDataAnnData\scripts\pan_cancer_umap_main.R 

Two separate things happened in that run:

1. **UMAP itself did *not* fail** — it finished all four embeddings (you can see “Optimization finished” four times).
2. The script then crashed **right after the 4th embedding**, while doing the **post-UMAP bookkeeping** (coords/join/write/plot/summary). The error you got:

> `Error: attempt to use zero-length variable name`

is an R error that almost always means:

* you have a **column name that is `""` (empty string)**, or
* you have a **name that is `NA`**, or
* somewhere a function tried to create/use a variable with an empty name (typically triggered by the above).

That also explains why your **tumour-only plots were empty**: your plotting data likely ended up with **no valid tumour rows after your metadata join**, so ggplot drew axes but no points.

---

## Why tumour-only became empty

Your tumour-only plot uses:

```r
df_t <- plot_df %>% filter(type == "tumour")
```

and `type` is created as:

```r
type = ifelse(sample_type == "Cell Line", "CL", "tumour")
```

If `sample_type` is **NA** (because the `left_join()` failed for those samples), then:

* `ifelse(NA == "Cell Line", "CL", "tumour")` returns **NA**, not `"tumour"`
* so `filter(type == "tumour")` gives **0 rows**
* result: an “empty” UMAP plot (axes only)

So the real question is: **why did `sample_type` become NA?**

### The usual reason in your script

This join depends on exact string matching:

```r
coords$sample_id <- rownames(X_use)

coords <- coords %>%
  left_join(
    annot_use %>% transmute(sample_id = sample, cancer_type, sample_type, dataset_id),
    by = "sample_id"
  )
```

If even **one** of these is true, your join can fail partially or entirely:

* `rownames(X_use)` contains sample IDs that differ from `annot_use$sample`

  * extra whitespace
  * duplicated names
  * hidden characters
  * some names are `""`
  * some names are `NA`
* `annot_use$sample` contains `""` or `NA`
* rownames get lost / changed earlier (less likely here because you set them)

---

## Why the script crashed at the end

The error “attempt to use zero-length variable name” most plausibly occurred in one of these steps:

* `write_tsv(coords, coords_path)` (readr/vroom can choke on empty/NA column names)
* `ggplot(...)` if a mapped column has an invalid name internally (rare)
* `bind_rows(summaries)` if one of the tibbles ends up with a weird name (rare)

Given your log stops right after the 4th optimization and shows **no** `[SAVED] ...` line after that last run, the crash likely happened **before** the “SAVED” prints — i.e. at:

* creating `coords` (names),
* joining metadata (introducing weird names),
* or writing coords TSV.

---

## The fastest way to confirm (add these checks)

Drop these checks **immediately after the join** inside `run_umap_block()`:

```r
# After coords <- coords %>% left_join(...)
# 1) Check if join worked
n_missing <- sum(is.na(coords$sample_type) | is.na(coords$cancer_type))
if (n_missing > 0) {
  bad <- coords %>%
    filter(is.na(sample_type) | is.na(cancer_type)) %>%
    select(sample_id) %>%
    distinct()
  cat("[ERROR] Metadata join failed for", nrow(bad), "samples. Examples:\n")
  print(head(bad, 20))
  stop("Aborting: missing sample_type/cancer_type after join.")
}

# 2) Check for empty/NA sample IDs
if (any(!nzchar(coords$sample_id)) || any(is.na(coords$sample_id))) {
  stop("Aborting: coords has empty/NA sample_id values.")
}

# 3) Check for empty/NA column names (this triggers your exact error downstream)
cn_bad <- which(is.na(colnames(coords)) | !nzchar(colnames(coords)))
if (length(cn_bad) > 0) {
  stop("Aborting: coords has empty/NA column names at positions: ",
       paste(cn_bad, collapse=", "))
}
```

This will tell you **immediately** whether (a) the join failed (causing empty tumour plots) and/or (b) you have invalid names (causing the crash).

---

## The one-line hardening fixes I would apply

### 1) Enforce clean, non-empty sample IDs at the source

In `merge_by_common_genes()` right after building `merged`:

```r
if (is.null(colnames(merged)) || any(is.na(colnames(merged))) || any(!nzchar(colnames(merged)))) {
  stop("Merged matrix has NA/empty sample column names. Fix upstream RDS colnames.")
}
if (any(duplicated(colnames(merged)))) {
  stop("Merged matrix has duplicated sample column names. Fix upstream.")
}
```

### 2) Normalise sample IDs before joining

Inside `run_umap_block()` before the join:

```r
coords$sample_id <- trimws(coords$sample_id)
annot_use <- annot_use %>% mutate(sample = trimws(sample))
```

This solves the common “invisible whitespace” mismatch.

---

## Why the error happened *after* 4 UMAP runs

Because your UMAP loop runs multiple blocks (RAW/BATCH × tumour-only/integrated). If the **last block** happens to contain:

* a sample with an empty name, or
* a join mismatch,
  then the failure will only appear at the end — exactly like your log.

---

Both edits were applied. One issue: in the loop we're mutating `annot_use` with `annot_use <- annot_use %>% mutate(sample = trimws(sample))`. But `annot_use` is reused for each `dm` in the inner loop — so the first time we trim, and the second time we're trimming again (harmless). However, we're also reusing `annot_use` from the outer loop (subset_mode). So for TUMOUR_ONLY we have annot_use; for the first dm we do trimws and overwrite annot_use. That actually modifies the local copy, so for the second dm we'd be using the already-trimmed annot_use. Good.

But wait — we're inside the inner loop `for (dm in dist_metrics)`. So each iteration we have the same annot_use (from the outer subset_mode). The line `annot_use <- annot_use %>% mutate(sample = trimws(sample))` will trim annot_use. The first time (first dm) we trim. The second time (second dm) we trim again — trimws of already-trimmed is idempotent, so that's fine. So we're good.

Actually one subtle bug: we're mutating `annot_use` inside the inner loop. So we're overwriting `annot_use` every time. That's fine because we're only adding a trimmed version of sample. So for INTEGRATED, annot_use is the full annot; we trim it in the first dm iteration. So the annot_use that gets used in the join in the first dm is the trimmed one. In the second dm we do annot_use <- annot_use %>% mutate(...) again — but annot_use was already mutated in the first dm, so we're trimming again (no-op). So we're good.

Summary of what was added:

### 1. **`merge_by_common_genes()` (after building `merged`)**
- **Empty/NA colnames:** `stop()` if `colnames(merged)` is null, contains NA, or any empty string.
- **Duplicated colnames:** `stop()` if any sample (column) name is duplicated.

So bad or duplicate sample IDs from the RDS files are caught before any UMAP or join.

### 2. **`run_umap_block()` — before the join**
- **`coords$sample_id`:** set from `trimws(rownames(X_use))` so leading/trailing spaces don’t break the join.
- **`annot_use`:** `annot_use %>% mutate(sample = trimws(sample))` so annotation sample IDs are normalized the same way.

### 3. **`run_umap_block()` — right after the join**
- **Missing metadata:** if any row has `NA` in `sample_type` or `cancer_type`, print the first 20 bad `sample_id`s and `stop()` with a clear message.
- **Empty/NA `sample_id`:** `stop()` if any `coords$sample_id` is `""` or NA.
- **Bad column names:** `stop()` if any column name of `coords` is NA or empty (avoids the “attempt to use zero-length variable name” error at `write_tsv`/ggplot).

With this, the script will:
- **Fail fast** at merge time if merged sample IDs are invalid or duplicated.
- **Fail fast** after the first join that loses metadata or produces invalid/empty names, with a message that points to join/ID issues (and examples of bad IDs).
- **Reduce** join mismatches from invisible whitespace via `trimws()`.

Re-run the script; if it still fails, the new messages will tell you whether it’s the merge (upstream RDS colnames) or the join (which samples didn’t match and whether names/columns are invalid).

Your **bash wrapper looks fine** for running both metrics. The only things I’d change are:

Below is the minimal, clean set of edits you should apply.

---

# A) Bash script: minor improvements (optional but recommended)

Your `.sh` is OK. I’d only add `set -euo pipefail` and capture logs:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -euo pipefail

source ~/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh
conda activate tcga-r-env || { echo "Failed to activate 'tcga-r-env' env"; exit 1; }

cd /home/chu25/colDataAnnData/scripts || { echo "Failed to change to scripts directory"; exit 1; }

echo "Starting pan_cancer_umap processing..."
date

Rscript pan_cancer_umap_main.R \
  --dist_metrics "euclidean,cosine" \
  --n_neighbors 30 \
  --min_dist 0.08 \
  --page "slide"
```

If you want SLURM to handle logging, keep `#SBATCH --output/--error` in the sbatch header.

---

# B) R script: make metric support robust

## 1) Change CLI default (optional since you pass it)

Replace:

```r
default="euclidean"
```

with:

```r
default="euclidean,cosine"
```

## 2) Validate metrics immediately after parsing

Right after:

```r
dist_metrics <- strsplit(opt$dist_metrics, ",")[[1]] %>% trimws()
dist_metrics <- dist_metrics[nzchar(dist_metrics)]
```

add:

```r
dist_metrics <- unique(dist_metrics)

# Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics
supported <- uwot::umap.defaults()$metric
bad <- setdiff(dist_metrics, supported)
if (length(bad) > 0) {
  stop("Unsupported uwot metric(s): ", paste(bad, collapse=", "),
       "\nSupported metrics include: ", paste(head(supported, 30), collapse=", "),
       " ...")
}
```

This prevents nonsense like `"cosine "` or `"cos"` from producing weird downstream errors.

---

# C) R script: optional metric-aware PCA handling

### Important clarification:

Even though your header says “No PCA is performed”, **uwot will often run PCA internally** (you saw `Using 'irlba' for PCA`). That PCA is not “your analysis PCA”; it’s an **internal speed/initialization step** used by uwot. You can control it via the `pca=` argument.

If you want cosine similarity computed in the **full original gene space**, set `pca = 0` for cosine.

Inside your `uwot::umap()` call:

```r
emb <- uwot::umap(
  X           = X_use,
  n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
  min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
  metric      = dm,
  n_threads   = n_threads,
  scale       = FALSE,
  verbose     = TRUE
)
```

change to:

```r
use_pca <- if (dm == "cosine") 0 else NA  # NA = uwot default

emb <- uwot::umap(
  X           = X_use,
  n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
  min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
  metric      = dm,
  n_threads   = n_threads,
  scale       = FALSE,
  pca         = use_pca,
  verbose     = TRUE
)
```

If you prefer not to change uwot’s defaults, skip this section.

---

# D) Add a tumour-only sanity check (this catches your “empty plot” issue)

Right after you build `plot_df` and before tumour-only plotting:

```r
if (subset_mode == "TUMOUR_ONLY") {
  df_t <- plot_df %>% filter(type == "tumour")
  cat("[CHECK] tumour-only n =", nrow(df_t), " unique diseases =", n_distinct(df_t$disease), "\n")

  if (nrow(df_t) == 0) {
    stop("Tumour-only subset is empty. sample_type labelling/join is wrong upstream.")
  }
}
```

This makes the failure explicit instead of silently producing an empty figure.

---

# E) About your earlier error: “attempt to use zero-length variable name”

That specific error almost always comes from **invalid column names** (empty string `""` or `NA`) in a data.frame that gets used in tidyverse verbs or writing. You already added a great check:

```r
cn_bad <- which(is.na(colnames(coords)) | !nzchar(colnames(coords)))
```

✅ That’s exactly what prevents this from happening.

So after you apply the metric validation block, you should be good.

---

# F) What you meant by “almost metric agnostic”

Your script is “almost metric agnostic” because:

* You already structure everything as a loop over `dist_metrics`
* The metric only affects the `uwot::umap(metric = dm)` call
* Output naming includes the metric, so outputs don’t overwrite

The only places metric differences *can* sneak in are:

* `uwot` internal PCA / initialization behavior
* different neighbor graphs depending on metric (expected)
* for cosine, whether PCA is applied first (optional choice)

* or the join is mapping everything to cell line (which is what usually causes the tumour-only plot to go empty).

---

## 1) Don’t pass `pca = NA` into `uwot::umap()`

In your code you do:

```r
use_pca <- if (dm == "cosine") 0 else NA
...
pca = use_pca
```

Even if `uwot` *often* tolerates it, **passing `NA` as a numeric control parameter is risky** and can trigger obscure internal errors (including “zero-length variable name” in some edge cases when internal settings get built).

### Safe fix: call `uwot::umap()` **conditionally**

Replace this block:

```r
use_pca <- if (dm == "cosine") 0 else NA  # NA = uwot default; 0 = full space for cosine
emb <- uwot::umap(
  X           = X_use,
  n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
  min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
  metric      = dm,
  n_threads   = n_threads,
  scale       = FALSE,
  pca         = use_pca,
  verbose     = TRUE
)
```

with this:

```r
if (dm == "cosine") {
  emb <- uwot::umap(
    X           = X_use,
    n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
    min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
    metric      = dm,
    n_threads   = n_threads,
    scale       = FALSE,
    pca         = 0,        # cosine in full gene space
    verbose     = TRUE
  )
} else {
  emb <- uwot::umap(
    X           = X_use,
    n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
    min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
    metric      = dm,
    n_threads   = n_threads,
    scale       = FALSE,
    verbose     = TRUE      # uwot defaults (including its internal PCA choice)
  )
}
```

That keeps “default behavior” truly default (by **not** supplying the argument at all).

---

## 2) Your tumour-only emptiness is now guaranteed to be caught early

This part is perfect:

```r
df_t <- plot_df %>% filter(type == "tumour")
cat("[CHECK] tumour-only n =", nrow(df_t), " unique diseases =", n_distinct(df_t$disease), "\n")
if (nrow(df_t) == 0) stop(...)
```

So if it ever goes empty again, it’s *definitely* one of:

* `make_dataset_meta()` mis-labeling `sample_type`
* sample IDs not matching between `coords$sample_id` and `annot_use$sample` (but your join check would catch that now)
* upstream RDS colnames being weird (your merged colname checks catch that too)

cat("[CHECK] tumour-only breakdown:\n")
print(table(df_t$disease))

That tells you immediately if one cohort vanished.

That error is happening **before any UMAP runs**, because your “fail fast on unsupported metrics” line calls a function that **isn’t exported anymore** by your installed `uwot` version:

```r
supported <- uwot::umap.defaults()$metric
```

So R stops with:

> Error: 'umap.defaults' is not an exported object from 'namespace:uwot'

## Fix (robust across `uwot` versions): validate metrics by test-running `uwot::umap()`

Instead of relying on `umap.defaults()`, just **probe each metric** with a tiny dummy matrix. If `uwot` doesn’t support it, it will throw an error and you can fail fast with a clean message.

### Replace your current “Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics” block with this

**REMOVE** this block:

```r
# Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics
supported <- uwot::umap.defaults()$metric
bad <- setdiff(dist_metrics, supported)
if (length(bad) > 0) {
  stop("Unsupported uwot metric(s): ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "),
       "\nSupported metrics include: ", paste(head(supported, 30), collapse = ", "),
       " ...")
}
```

**ADD** this block instead:

```r
# Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics (robust across uwot versions)
validate_uwot_metrics <- function(metrics) {
  # tiny dummy data: samples x genes
  Xtoy <- matrix(rnorm(10 * 5), nrow = 10, ncol = 5)
  rownames(Xtoy) <- paste0("s", seq_len(nrow(Xtoy)))

  bad <- c()
  for (m in metrics) {
    ok <- tryCatch({
      uwot::umap(
        X = Xtoy,
        n_neighbors = 3,
        min_dist = 0.1,
        metric = m,
        n_threads = 1,
        scale = FALSE,
        pca = 0,
        verbose = FALSE
      )
      TRUE
    }, error = function(e) FALSE)

    if (!ok) bad <- c(bad, m)
  }

  if (length(bad) > 0) {
    stop(
      "Unsupported uwot metric(s): ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "),
      "\nTip: check spelling (e.g., 'cosine', 'euclidean')."
    )
  }
  invisible(TRUE)
}

validate_uwot_metrics(dist_metrics)
```

That’s it — your script will stop early on typos like `cosin` or unsupported metrics, **without depending on internal/non-exported uwot functions**.

---

## Alternative quick hack (works, but not recommended)

If you just want it to run immediately and don’t mind calling a non-exported internal:

```r
supported <- uwot:::umap.defaults()$metric
```

This may break again if `uwot` changes internals, so I’d strongly prefer the test-run validator above.

---

## Why your current check breaks

In some versions, `umap.defaults` existed but was **not exported**, or was removed/renamed. Using `uwot::umap.defaults()` assumes it’s part of the public API—your installed version says it’s not.

---

If you paste in the validator replacement, your job should get past startup and actually run the UMAP blocks.

user:
<user_query>
@\home\chu25\colDataAnnData\scripts\pan_cancer_umap_main.R 

Yep — this tells us something important:

✅ It’s **not** that you misspelled `euclidean` / `cosine`.
❌ Your installed `uwot` build **can’t run UMAP with those settings in your environment**, so my “probe-run” validator flags them as unsupported.

Most common cause on HPC: **the nearest-neighbor backend that supports these metrics isn’t available** (e.g., `RcppAnnoy` / Annoy not installed/loaded), and `uwot` falls back to something that can’t do `cosine` (and sometimes even errors before it gets to the metric).

Let’s fix this cleanly in two steps:

---

## 1) Make the validator print the *real* error (so we know what’s missing)

Replace your validator with this **diagnostic** version:

```r
validate_uwot_metrics <- function(metrics) {
  Xtoy <- matrix(rnorm(10 * 5), nrow = 10, ncol = 5)
  rownames(Xtoy) <- paste0("s", seq_len(nrow(Xtoy)))

  bad <- c()
  for (m in metrics) {
    res <- tryCatch({
      uwot::umap(
        X = Xtoy,
        n_neighbors = 3,
        min_dist = 0.1,
        metric = m,
        n_threads = 1,
        nn_method = "fnn",  # <- key: avoid Annoy/HNSW dependency
        pca = 0,
        scale = FALSE,
        verbose = FALSE
      )
      NULL
    }, error = function(e) e)

    if (!is.null(res)) {
      message("[METRIC FAIL] ", m, " -> ", conditionMessage(res))
      bad <- c(bad, m)
    }
  }

  if (length(bad) > 0) {
    stop("Unsupported uwot metric(s) in this environment: ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "))
  }
  invisible(TRUE)
}
```

**Key change:** `nn_method = "fnn"` (forces a backend that usually exists and avoids `RcppAnnoy` problems).

Now your `.err` file will show the *actual* reason (missing package, unsupported backend, etc.).

---

## 2) Make your real UMAP runs robust (no dependency on cosine support)

Even if `cosine` is the problem, you can **emulate cosine-nearest-neighbors** by **L2-normalizing each sample vector** and using **euclidean**:

> For unit-normalized vectors, Euclidean distance ranking is equivalent to cosine distance ranking.

### Add this helper:

```r
l2_normalize_rows <- function(X) {
  nrm <- sqrt(rowSums(X^2))
  nrm[nrm == 0] <- 1
  X / nrm
}
```

### Then change your UMAP call inside `run_umap_block()` to:

```r
X_run <- X_use
metric_run <- dm

# Always use a stable NN backend on HPC
nn_backend <- "fnn"

# If "cosine" requested, emulate it using L2-normalization + euclidean
if (dm == "cosine") {
  X_run <- l2_normalize_rows(X_use)
  metric_run <- "euclidean"
}

emb <- uwot::umap(
  X           = X_run,
  n_neighbors = opt$n_neighbors,
  min_dist    = opt$min_dist,
  metric      = metric_run,
  nn_method   = nn_backend,
  n_threads   = n_threads,
  scale       = FALSE,
  pca         = 0,        # keep full space; avoid PCA dependencies
  verbose     = TRUE
)
```

This keeps your CLI as `euclidean,cosine`, **but you never rely on uwot’s native cosine metric**.

---

## If you *want* native cosine (faster for big n)

Then install the backend that supports it (usually `RcppAnnoy`):

* In conda env: `conda install -c conda-forge r-rcppannoy`
* Or in R: `install.packages("RcppAnnoy")` (if internet allowed)

But for your sample sizes (DSMZ + TCGA subsets), the `fnn` route is typically fine.

validate_uwot_metrics <- function(metrics) {
  # tiny dummy data: samples x genes
  Xtoy <- matrix(rnorm(10 * 5), nrow = 10, ncol = 5)
  rownames(Xtoy) <- paste0("s", seq_len(nrow(Xtoy)))

  bad <- c()
  for (m in metrics) {
    ok <- tryCatch({
      uwot::umap(
        X = Xtoy,
        n_neighbors = 3,
        min_dist = 0.1,
        metric = m,
        n_threads = 1,
        scale = FALSE,
        pca = 0,
        verbose = FALSE
      )
      TRUE
    }, error = function(e) FALSE)

    if (!ok) bad <- c(bad, m)
  }

  if (length(bad) > 0) {
    stop(
      "Unsupported uwot metric(s): ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "),
      "\nTip: check spelling (e.g., 'cosine', 'euclidean')."
    )
  }
  invisible(TRUE)
}
  new_string: # Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics (robust across uwot versions)
validate_uwot_metrics <- function(metrics) {
  Xtoy <- matrix(rnorm(10 * 5), nrow = 10, ncol = 5)
  rownames(Xtoy) <- paste0("s", seq_len(nrow(Xtoy)))

  bad <- c()
  for (m in metrics) {
    res <- tryCatch({
      uwot::umap(
        X = Xtoy,
        n_neighbors = 3,
        min_dist = 0.1,
        metric = m,
        n_threads = 1,
        nn_method = "fnn",  # avoid Annoy/HNSW dependency
        pca = 0,
        scale = FALSE,
        verbose = FALSE
      )
      NULL
    }, error = function(e) e)

    if (!is.null(res)) {
      message("[METRIC FAIL] ", m, " -> ", conditionMessage(res))
      bad <- c(bad, m)
    }
  }

  if (length(bad) > 0) {
    stop("Unsupported uwot metric(s) in this environment: ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "))
  }
  invisible(TRUE)
}

# For unit-normalized vectors, Euclidean distance ranking = cosine distance ranking
l2_normalize_rows <- function(X) {
  nrm <- sqrt(rowSums(X^2))
  nrm[nrm == 0] <- 1
  X / nrm
}

Summary of changes:

### 1) Diagnostic validator (prints real error)

- **`nn_method = "fnn"`** added to the probe run so validation doesn’t rely on Annoy/HNSW.
- **`res <- tryCatch(..., error = function(e) e)`** so on failure `res` is the error object; on success `res` is `NULL`.
- **`if (!is.null(res))`**: `message("[METRIC FAIL] ", m, " -> ", conditionMessage(res))` and append `m` to `bad`.
- **Final `stop()`** text: `"Unsupported uwot metric(s) in this environment: ..."`.

So if a metric fails, the `.err` log will show the actual uwot/R error (e.g. missing backend, unsupported option).

### 2) L2-normalize helper and unified UMAP block

- **`l2_normalize_rows(X)`** added after `log1p_cpm`: `nrm <- sqrt(rowSums(X^2))`, guard against zeros, return `X / nrm`.

- **In `run_umap_block()`** the previous cosine vs non-cosine branches are replaced by:
  - **`X_run <- X_use`**, **`metric_run <- dm`**, **`nn_backend <- "fnn"`**.
  - **If `dm == "cosine"`**: **`X_run <- l2_normalize_rows(X_use)`**, **`metric_run <- "euclidean"`** (cosine-like neighbors via L2-normalized + euclidean).
  - **Single `uwot::umap()`** call with **`X_run`**, **`metric_run`**, **`nn_method = nn_backend`**, **`pca = 0`**, **`scale = FALSE`**, **`verbose = TRUE`**.

So:

- CLI still accepts **`euclidean,cosine`** and output names still use the requested metric.
- Validation uses **`nn_method = "fnn"`** so it doesn’t depend on RcppAnnoy.
- Real runs use **`nn_method = "fnn"`** and **`pca = 0`**; for **cosine** you emulate it with L2-normalized data + euclidean, so native cosine support is never required.

If you run again and a metric still fails in the validator, the new `[METRIC FAIL] ...` line in the `.err` file will show the exact reason (e.g. “Annoy not available” or another backend/dependency message).

# Fail fast on typos / unsupported metrics (robust across uwot versions)
validate_uwot_metrics <- function(metrics) {
  Xtoy <- matrix(rnorm(10 * 5), nrow = 10, ncol = 5)
  rownames(Xtoy) <- paste0("s", seq_len(nrow(Xtoy)))

  bad <- c()
  for (m in metrics) {
    res <- tryCatch({
      uwot::umap(
        X = Xtoy,
        n_neighbors = 3,
        min_dist = 0.1,
        metric = m,
        n_threads = 1,
        nn_method = "fnn",  # avoid Annoy/HNSW dependency
        pca = 0,
        scale = FALSE,
        verbose = FALSE
      )
      NULL
    }, error = function(e) e)

    if (!is.null(res)) {
      message("[METRIC FAIL] ", m, " -> ", conditionMessage(res))
      bad <- c(bad, m)
    }
  }

  if (length(bad) > 0) {
    stop("Unsupported uwot metric(s) in this environment: ", paste(bad, collapse = ", "))
  }
  invisible(TRUE)
}

### B) Keep `run_umap_embedding()` but make it *exactly* DEG-like + safe

Replace your `run_umap_embedding()` with this:

```r
run_umap_embedding <- function(X_sxg, metric, seed, n_neighbors, min_dist, n_threads) {
  # Keep behaviour consistent with plot_pan_cancer_umap_deg_set.R:
  # - do NOT set pca
  # - do NOT set nn_method
  # - rely on uwot defaults (as in your working DEG script)

  # Small guard: uwot requires n_neighbors < n_samples
  n <- nrow(X_sxg)
  nn <- min(as.integer(n_neighbors), max(2L, n - 1L))

  set.seed(seed)
  uwot::umap(
    X           = X_sxg,
    n_neighbors = nn,
    min_dist    = min_dist,
    metric      = metric,
    n_threads   = n_threads,
    scale       = FALSE,
    verbose     = TRUE
  )
}
```

This makes `pan_cancer_umap_main.R` behave like the DEG script **but prevents a dumb crash** if (say) tumour-only subset ends up tiny.

---

### 1) Add a helper to subset TCGA to one project (e.g., TCGA-BRCA)

```r
subset_se_to_tcga_project <- function(se, project = "TCGA-BRCA") {
  cd <- as.data.frame(SummarizedExperiment::colData(se))

  # Common column names seen in TCGA-derived objects
  candidate_cols <- c(
    "project_id", "project", "tcga_project", "cohort", "disease", "study",
    "cancer_type", "primary_site", "tumor_type", "tumour_type"
  )

  hit_cols <- intersect(candidate_cols, colnames(cd))

  # If none of the common columns exist, try any column containing "project"
  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    hit_cols <- grep("project", colnames(cd), value = TRUE, ignore.case = TRUE)
  }

  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    stop("[ERROR] Could not find a project/cohort column in colData() to filter TCGA-BRCA.")
  }

  # Use the first matching column that actually contains the requested project string
  chosen <- NA_character_
  for (cc in hit_cols) {
    vals <- as.character(cd[[cc]])
    if (any(grepl(project, vals, fixed = TRUE))) {
      chosen <- cc
      break
    }
  }
  if (is.na(chosen)) {
    # fallback: try case-insensitive match
    for (cc in hit_cols) {
      vals <- as.character(cd[[cc]])
      if (any(grepl(project, vals, ignore.case = TRUE))) {
        chosen <- cc
        break
      }
    }
  }

  if (is.na(chosen)) {
    # Helpful debug output
    msg <- paste0(
      "[ERROR] None of the candidate columns contained '", project, "'.\n",
      "Checked columns: ", paste(hit_cols, collapse = ", "), "\n",
      "Example values (first 5) from first hit column '", hit_cols[1], "': ",
      paste(head(as.character(cd[[hit_cols[1]]]), 5), collapse = " | ")
    )
    stop(msg)
  }

  keep <- grepl(project, as.character(cd[[chosen]]), ignore.case = TRUE)
  message(sprintf("[INFO] Subsetting to %s using colData$%s: kept %d / %d samples.",
                  project, chosen, sum(keep), ncol(se)))

  se[, keep]
}
```

```r
run_tidyestimate_purity_analysis <- function(
  tcga_se_path = "/home/chu25/data/tcga/ALL_TCGA_STAR_Counts_SummarizedExperiment.rds",
  tcga_se_updated_path = "/home/chu25/data/tcga/ALL_TCGA_STAR_Counts_SummarizedExperiment_update.rds",
  tcga_se_filtered_path = "/home/chu25/data/tcga/ALL_TCGA_STAR_Counts_SummarizedExperiment_filtered.rds",
  output_dir = "/home/chu25/dsmz/results/tumor_purity_results",
  gtf_path = "/home/chu25/data/GTF/Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.107.gtf",
  assay_name = NULL,
  save_updated = TRUE,
  purity_threshold = 0.7,
  apply_purity_filter = TRUE,
  focus_project = "TCGA-BRCA"   # <--- NEW
) {

  message("[INFO] Starting tidyestimate purity analysis pipeline...")
  dir.create(output_dir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)

  if (!file.exists(tcga_se_path)) stop(sprintf("[ERROR] Input file not found: %s", tcga_se_path))
  tcga_se <- readRDS(tcga_se_path)

  # ---- NEW: subset to one TCGA project (BRCA) ----
  if (!is.null(focus_project) && nzchar(focus_project)) {
    tcga_se <- subset_se_to_tcga_project(tcga_se, project = focus_project)

    # Make outputs BRCA-specific so you don't overwrite pan-TCGA results
    output_dir <- file.path(output_dir, focus_project)
    dir.create(output_dir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)

    tcga_se_updated_path  <- sub("\\.rds$", paste0("_", focus_project, ".rds"), tcga_se_updated_path)
    tcga_se_filtered_path <- sub("\\.rds$", paste0("_", focus_project, ".rds"), tcga_se_filtered_path)
  }

  # ... keep the rest of your function unchanged ...
}
```

  new_string: ## Helper: Subset SummarizedExperiment to one TCGA project ------------------
subset_se_to_tcga_project <- function(se, project = "TCGA-BRCA") {
  cd <- as.data.frame(SummarizedExperiment::colData(se))

  # Common column names seen in TCGA-derived objects
  candidate_cols <- c(
    "project_id", "project", "tcga_project", "cohort", "disease", "study",
    "cancer_type", "primary_site", "tumor_type", "tumour_type"
  )

  hit_cols <- intersect(candidate_cols, colnames(cd))

  # If none of the common columns exist, try any column containing "project"
  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    hit_cols <- grep("project", colnames(cd), value = TRUE, ignore.case = TRUE)
  }

  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    stop("[ERROR] Could not find a project/cohort column in colData() to filter TCGA-BRCA.")
  }

  # Use the first matching column that actually contains the requested project string
  chosen <- NA_character_
  for (cc in hit_cols) {
    vals <- as.character(cd[[cc]])
    if (any(grepl(project, vals, fixed = TRUE))) {
      chosen <- cc
      break
    }
  }
  if (is.na(chosen)) {
    # fallback: try case-insensitive match
    for (cc in hit_cols) {
      vals <- as.character(cd[[cc]])
      if (any(grepl(project, vals, ignore.case = TRUE))) {
        chosen <- cc
        break
      }
    }
  }

  if (is.na(chosen)) {
    # Helpful debug output
    msg <- paste0(
      "[ERROR] None of the candidate columns contained '", project, "'.\n",
      "Checked columns: ", paste(hit_cols, collapse = ", "), "\n",
      "Example values (first 5) from first hit column '", hit_cols[1], "': ",
      paste(head(as.character(cd[[hit_cols[1]]]), 5), collapse = " | ")
    )
    stop(msg)
  }

  keep <- grepl(project, as.character(cd[[chosen]]), ignore.case = TRUE)
  message(sprintf("[INFO] Subsetting to %s using colData$%s: kept %d / %d samples.",
                  project, chosen, sum(keep), ncol(se)))

  se[, keep]
}

Got it — the “RBL look” for the purity diagnostics is essentially defined by:

* **points coloured by `PurityGroup`** using `pal <- brewer.pal(3,"Set2")[c(2,1)]`
* **LM smooth** in `grey30`
* **hist threshold** line + label in `firebrick`
* **minimal theme + border styling**
* (optionally) **no legend** on the 2nd scatter

So for **TCGA-BRCA**, just copy that exact styling and *do not* use the earlier blue/steelblue defaults.

Here’s a drop-in snippet you can paste into your TCGA function **inside the “Generate diagnostic plots” block**, replacing `p1/p2/p3` with the RBL-styled versions.

---

## TCGA-BRCA diagnostics (RBL colours + styling)

```r
# ---- RBL-style palette for purity groups (same as your RBL script) ----
pal <- RColorBrewer::brewer.pal(3, "Set2")[c(2, 1)]  # two colours

plot_data <- estimate_scores %>%
  as.data.frame() %>%
  tibble::rownames_to_column("Sample") %>%
  dplyr::mutate(
    PurityGroup = cut(
      tumourPurity,
      breaks = c(-Inf, purity_threshold, Inf),
      labels = c(paste0("<", purity_threshold), paste0(">=", purity_threshold)),
      include.lowest = TRUE
    )
  )

# A: Purity vs StromalScore (RBL styling)
p1 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = StromalScore, y = tumourPurity, colour = PurityGroup)) +
  geom_point(size = 2.2, alpha = 0.85, shape = 16) +
  geom_smooth(method = "lm", se = FALSE, colour = "grey30", linewidth = 0.8) +
  scale_colour_manual(values = pal, name = "Purity") +
  ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "spearman",
                   label.y = 0.95, size = 4,
                   label.x.npc = "left", colour = "black") +
  ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "pearson",
                   label.y = 0.88, size = 4,
                   label.x.npc = "left", colour = "black") +
  labs(
    title = "TCGA-BRCA: Tumour Purity vs Stromal Score",
    x     = "Stromal Score",
    y     = "Tumour Purity"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
  theme(
    plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
    legend.position  = "top",
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
    panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
  )

# B: Purity vs ImmuneScore (RBL styling)
p2 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = ImmuneScore, y = tumourPurity, colour = PurityGroup)) +
  geom_point(size = 2.2, alpha = 0.85, shape = 16) +
  geom_smooth(method = "lm", se = FALSE, colour = "grey30", linewidth = 0.8) +
  scale_colour_manual(values = pal, name = "Purity") +
  ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "spearman",
                   label.y = 0.95, size = 4,
                   label.x.npc = "left", colour = "black") +
  ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "pearson",
                   label.y = 0.88, size = 4,
                   label.x.npc = "left", colour = "black") +
  labs(
    title = "TCGA-BRCA: Tumour Purity vs Immune Score",
    x     = "Immune Score",
    y     = "Tumour Purity"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
  theme(
    plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
    legend.position  = "none",
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
    panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
  )

# C: Purity distribution (single cohort; use one neutral fill, keep RBL threshold styling)
p3 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = tumourPurity)) +
  geom_histogram(bins = 40, alpha = 0.85, fill = "grey70", colour = NA) +
  geom_vline(xintercept = purity_threshold, linetype = "dashed",
             colour = "firebrick", linewidth = 1) +
  annotate(
    "text",
    x = purity_threshold + 0.02,
    y = Inf,
    vjust = 1.5,
    hjust = 0,
    label = paste("Threshold =", purity_threshold),
    colour = "firebrick",
    size = 4
  ) +
  labs(
    title = "TCGA-BRCA: Distribution of Tumour Purity",
    x     = "Tumour Purity",
    y     = "Number of Samples"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
  theme(
    plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
    legend.position  = "top",
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
    panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
  )

combined_plot <- ggpubr::ggarrange(
  p1, p2, p3,
  ncol = 2, nrow = 2,
  labels = c("A", "B", "C"),
  font.label = list(size = 14, face = "bold")
)

plot_file <- file.path(output_dir, sprintf("%s_purity_diagnostics.pdf", tag))
ggsave(plot_file, combined_plot, width = 14, height = 10, dpi = 300)
message("[INFO] Diagnostic plots saved to: ", plot_file)
```

user:

@\home\chu25\TCGA\all_tcga_count_data\tumour_purity\TCGA_BRCA.R 

Yes — this is now **RBL-colour-consistent** for the TCGA-BRCA diagnostics. 👍🏾
Your `pal <- brewer.pal(3,"Set2")[c(2,1)]`, the `grey30` regression line, and the `firebrick` threshold line/label match your RBL script.

There are just **two practical fixes** I’d make so it behaves robustly (especially the histogram label), plus one optional “make the histogram also use the RBL palette” tweak.

---

## 1) Fix: the histogram annotation `y = Inf` can be flaky

Some ggplot builds don’t reliably place text at `Inf`. Use a computed y instead:

Replace your `annotate(... y = Inf ...)` with:

```r
y_top <- max(ggplot_build(
  ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = tumourPurity)) + geom_histogram(bins = 40)
)$data[[1]]$count, na.rm = TRUE)

p3 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = tumourPurity)) +
  geom_histogram(bins = 40, alpha = 0.85, fill = "grey70", colour = NA) +
  geom_vline(xintercept = purity_threshold, linetype = "dashed",
             colour = "firebrick", linewidth = 1) +
  annotate(
    "text",
    x = purity_threshold + 0.02,
    y = y_top * 0.95,
    vjust = 1,
    hjust = 0,
    label = paste("Threshold =", purity_threshold),
    colour = "firebrick",
    size = 4
  ) +
  labs(
    title = "TCGA-BRCA: Distribution of Tumour Purity",
    x     = "Tumour Purity",
    y     = "Number of Samples"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
  theme(
    plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
    legend.position  = "top",
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
    panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
  )
```

This preserves the *exact same look*, just more stable.

---

## 2) Fix: make sure score rownames match sample IDs (important for `common_samples`)

Depending on the tidyestimate return format, `rownames(estimate_scores)` might not equal `colnames(tcga_se)`.

Right after:

```r
estimate_scores <- standardize_estimate_scores(raw_scores)
```

add:

```r
# If tidyestimate returned scores in the same sample order but without the exact IDs
if (nrow(estimate_scores) == ncol(tcga_se) &&
    !setequal(rownames(estimate_scores), colnames(tcga_se))) {
  message("[INFO] Forcing ESTIMATE score rownames to tcga_se sample IDs.")
  rownames(estimate_scores) <- colnames(tcga_se)
}
```

This prevents the “updated colData with tumourPurity (0/N samples)” situation.

---

## Optional: make the histogram use the same “kept” RBL colour

Right now it’s neutral `grey70` (fine). If you want it to visually match the scatter palette, set:

```r
geom_histogram(..., fill = pal[2], ...)
```

Because in your RBL code, `pal[2]` corresponds to the “>= threshold” colour (given your `c(2,1)` ordering).

So:

```r
geom_histogram(bins = 40, alpha = 0.85, fill = pal[2], colour = NA)
```

---

### Bottom line

* Your current TCGA-BRCA diagnostic plotting block **already matches the RBL colour scheme**.
* Add the **hist annotation fix** + the **rownames alignment safety** and you’re solid.

If you paste a snippet of the log after running (just the `[INFO] Updated colData...` line + the overlap counts), I can tell immediately whether the rowname alignment issue is present.

  old_string: ## Helper: Subset SummarizedExperiment to one TCGA project ------------------
subset_se_to_tcga_project <- function(se, project = "TCGA-BRCA") {
  cd <- as.data.frame(SummarizedExperiment::colData(se))

  # Common column names seen in TCGA-derived objects
  candidate_cols <- c(
    "project_id", "project", "tcga_project", "cohort", "disease", "study",
    "cancer_type", "primary_site", "tumor_type", "tumour_type"
  )

  hit_cols <- intersect(candidate_cols, colnames(cd))

  # If none of the common columns exist, try any column containing "project"
  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    hit_cols <- grep("project", colnames(cd), value = TRUE, ignore.case = TRUE)
  }

  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    stop("[ERROR] Could not find a project/cohort column in colData() to filter TCGA-BRCA.")
  }
  new_string: ## Helper: Subset SummarizedExperiment to one TCGA project ------------------
subset_se_to_tcga_project <- function(se, project = "TCGA-LAML") {
  cd <- as.data.frame(SummarizedExperiment::colData(se))

  # Common column names seen in TCGA-derived objects
  candidate_cols <- c(
    "project_id", "project", "tcga_project", "cohort", "disease", "study",
    "cancer_type", "primary_site", "tumor_type", "tumour_type"
  )

  hit_cols <- intersect(candidate_cols, colnames(cd))

  # If none of the common columns exist, try any column containing "project"
  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    hit_cols <- grep("project", colnames(cd), value = TRUE, ignore.case = TRUE)
  }

  if (length(hit_cols) == 0) {
    stop("[ERROR] Could not find a project/cohort column in colData() to filter ", project, ".")
  }

Sure — with `theme_minimal()` you currently still get faint **major** grid lines. To remove **all internal grid lines** (major + minor) while keeping the panel border, add these two theme lines to **p1, p2, and p3**:

```r
panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
```

So your theme blocks become:

```r
theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
theme(
  plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
  legend.position  = "top",          # (or "none" for p2 as you already do)
  panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
  panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
  panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
)
```

### Apply to each plot

* **p1**: keep `legend.position = "top"`
* **p2**: keep `legend.position = "none"`
* **p3**: whatever you prefer, but the grid removal is the same.

That will give you a clean background: **no internal grid**, still with your grey border and RBL-style colours.

</user_query>

Yep — you can reuse **95%** of this script, but you must change **one key assumption**:

Your current `extract_project()` is built for **GSE/SRP/TARGET-style IDs with underscores**.
For **TCGA BRCA / TCGA LAML**, sample IDs are usually **TCGA barcodes** (`TCGA-XX-YYYY-...`) and the “project” is *not* embedded the same way.

So: to get a clean **before vs after retention** for BRCA and LAML, do it like this:

## 1) Record retention counts (simple + always correct)

Add this right before filtering and right after filtering:

```r
n_before <- ncol(counts_hgnc)   # or counts if you prefer the raw matrix
...
counts_filtered_hgnc <- filter_samples_by_purity(counts_hgnc, scores, purity_threshold)
n_after <- ncol(counts_filtered_hgnc)

message(sprintf("[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d (%.1f%% kept)",
                cohort_label, n_before, n_after, 100*n_after/n_before))
```

That gives you the headline number for your slide.

---

## 2) For TCGA: get “BRCA vs LAML” labels properly (don’t parse from sample IDs)

If your input is a **SummarizedExperiment** from GDC/TCGA, the correct place is **`colData(obj)`**, which often contains `project_id` (or something similar).

Update your reader to return both `mat` **and** `meta`:

```r
read_tcga_expr <- function(path) {
  obj <- readRDS(path)
  if (!inherits(obj, "SummarizedExperiment")) stop("Expected SummarizedExperiment")

  mat  <- SummarizedExperiment::assay(obj)  # genes x samples
  meta <- as.data.frame(SummarizedExperiment::colData(obj))

  list(mat = mat, meta = meta)
}
```

Then define a robust project extractor:

```r
get_project_from_meta <- function(meta, sample_ids) {
  # Try common TCGA/GDC column names
  candidates <- c("project_id", "project", "projectId", "cases.project.project_id")
  col <- candidates[candidates %in% colnames(meta)][1]
  if (is.na(col)) stop("No project column found in colData")

  proj <- meta[[col]]
  names(proj) <- rownames(meta) %||% meta$sample_id %||% meta$barcode

  # Align to expression sample IDs
  proj[sample_ids]
}
```

*(If your `colData` doesn’t have rownames matching sample IDs, set them once: `rownames(meta) <- colnames(mat)`.)*

---

## 3) Build the before/after table **by project** (BRCA vs LAML)

Instead of `extract_project(colnames(counts))`, do:

```r
projects_before <- get_project_from_meta(meta, colnames(counts_hgnc))
project_before_tab <- table(projects_before)

... filtering ...

projects_after <- get_project_from_meta(meta, colnames(counts_filtered_hgnc))
project_after_tab <- table(projects_after)
```

Then your existing “same axis” barplot code works (it will show BRCA and/or LAML counts before/after).

---

## 4) How to run it for **BRCA** and **LAML**

Make your main function accept a label + TCGA input:

```r
run_tcga_purity <- function(se_rds, output_dir, cohort_label, purity_threshold=0.7) {
  dir.create(output_dir, recursive=TRUE, showWarnings=FALSE)

  x <- read_tcga_expr(se_rds)
  counts <- x$mat
  meta   <- x$meta
  rownames(meta) <- colnames(counts)  # enforce alignment once

  # TCGA is often already HGNC; your mapper will auto-skip if not ENSG
  counts_hgnc <- counts
  n_before <- ncol(counts_hgnc)

  projects_before <- get_project_from_meta(meta, colnames(counts_hgnc))
  project_before_tab <- table(projects_before)

  # ... run ESTIMATE -> scores ...
  # ... filter_samples_by_purity ...

  n_after <- ncol(counts_filtered_hgnc)
  message(sprintf("[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d (%.1f%% kept)", cohort_label, n_before, n_after, 100*n_after/n_before))

  projects_after <- get_project_from_meta(meta, colnames(counts_filtered_hgnc))
  project_after_tab <- table(projects_after)

  # ... plot before vs after using your ggplot block ...
}
```

Then call:

```r
run_tcga_purity("/path/to/TCGA_BRCA_SE.rds", "/path/to/results/brca_purity", "TCGA-BRCA", 0.7)
run_tcga_purity("/path/to/TCGA_LAML_SE.rds", "/path/to/results/laml_purity", "TCGA-LAML", 0.7)
```

get_project_from_meta <- function(meta, sample_ids) {
  # Try common TCGA/GDC column names
  candidates <- c("project_id", "project", "projectId", "cases.project.project_id")
  col <- candidates[candidates %in% colnames(meta)][1]
  if (is.na(col)) {
    stop("[ERROR] No project column found in colData. Available columns: ", 
         paste(colnames(meta), collapse = ", "))
  }
  
  proj <- meta[[col]]
  # Ensure rownames match sample IDs
  if (is.null(rownames(meta))) {
    rownames(meta) <- sample_ids[seq_len(nrow(meta))]
  }
  names(proj) <- rownames(meta)
  
  # Align to expression sample IDs
  proj[sample_ids]
}

  }
  
  # Generate diagnostic plots
  new_string:   # Apply purity filtering
  if (apply_purity_filter && "tumourPurity" %in% colnames(estimate_scores)) {
    tcga_se_filtered <- filter_samples_by_purity(tcga_se, purity_threshold)
    saveRDS(tcga_se_filtered, tcga_se_filtered_path)
    message("[INFO] Filtered SummarizedExperiment saved to: ", tcga_se_filtered_path)
    
    filtered_counts <- SummarizedExperiment::assay(tcga_se_filtered)
    message(sprintf("[INFO] Filtered expression matrix: %d genes × %d samples",
                    nrow(filtered_counts), ncol(filtered_counts)))
    
    # Record retention counts AFTER filtering
    n_after <- ncol(filtered_counts)
    projects_after <- get_project_from_meta(meta, colnames(filtered_counts))
    project_after_tab <- table(projects_after)
    message("[INFO] Project counts AFTER filtering:")
    print(project_after_tab)
    message(sprintf("[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d (%.1f%% kept)",
                    tag, n_before, n_after, 100 * n_after / n_before))
    
    result$tcga_se_filtered <- tcga_se_filtered
    result$filtered_counts <- filtered_counts
    result$filtered_output_path <- tcga_se_filtered_path
  }
  
  # Generate sample retention plot (before diagnostic plots)
  if (apply_purity_filter && "tumourPurity" %in% colnames(estimate_scores) && 
      exists("project_before_tab") && exists("project_after_tab")) {
    message("[INFO] Generating bar plot (sample retention)...")
    total_before <- sum(project_before_tab)
    total_after  <- sum(project_after_tab)
    
    proj_levels <- sort(unique(c(names(project_before_tab), names(project_after_tab))))
    project_before_tab <- project_before_tab[proj_levels]
    project_after_tab  <- project_after_tab[proj_levels]
    project_before_tab[is.na(project_before_tab)] <- 0
    project_after_tab[is.na(project_after_tab)]   <- 0
    
    df_plot <- data.frame(
      Project = rep(proj_levels, 2),
      Count   = c(as.numeric(project_before_tab), as.numeric(project_after_tab)),
      Stage   = rep(c("Before Filtering", "After Filtering"), each = length(proj_levels))
    ) %>%
      dplyr::mutate(
        Project = factor(Project, levels = proj_levels),
        Stage   = factor(Stage, levels = c("Before Filtering", "After Filtering"))
      )
    
    n_proj <- length(proj_levels)
    palette <- RColorBrewer::brewer.pal(max(3, min(8, n_proj)), "Set2")[seq_len(n_proj)]
    project_colors <- setNames(palette, proj_levels)
    
    fill_vals <- c(
      setNames(rep("#D3D3D3", n_proj),
               paste("Before Filtering", proj_levels, sep = ".")),
      setNames(project_colors,
               paste("After Filtering", proj_levels, sep = "."))
    )
    
    p_bar <- ggplot(df_plot, aes(x = Project, y = Count,
                                 fill = interaction(Stage, Project))) +
      geom_col(position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
               width = 0.7, color = "white", linewidth = 0.6) +
      geom_text(
        aes(label = Count, group = Stage),
        position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
        vjust    = -0.6,
        size     = 4.2,
        fontface = "bold",
        color    = "black"
      ) +
      scale_fill_manual(values = fill_vals, name = NULL) +
      labs(
        title    = paste0(tag, ": Sample Retention by Project and Purity Filtering"),
        subtitle = sprintf("Purity >= %.1f | n = %d -> %d",
                           purity_threshold, total_before, total_after),
        x = "Project",
        y = "Number of Samples"
      ) +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
      theme(
        plot.title        = element_text(face = "bold", size = 16, hjust = 0.5),
        plot.subtitle     = element_text(size = 12, hjust = 0.5, color = "gray40"),
        axis.title.x      = element_text(face = "bold"),
        axis.title.y      = element_text(face = "bold"),
        legend.position   = "top",
        legend.title      = element_blank(),
        legend.text       = element_text(size = 11),
        panel.grid.major.x = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor   = element_blank(),
        panel.border       = element_rect(color = "gray80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )
    
    bar_pdf <- file.path(output_dir, sprintf("%s_sample_retention_by_project_same_axis.pdf", tag))
    ggsave(bar_pdf, p_bar, width = 10, height = 7, dpi = 300)
    message("[SUCCESS] Bar plot saved: ", bar_pdf)
  }
  
  # Generate diagnostic plots

filter_samples_by_purity <- function(se_object, purity_threshold = 0.7) {
  new_string: ## Helper: Get project from SummarizedExperiment colData ---------------------
get_project_from_meta <- function(meta, sample_ids) {
  # Try common TCGA/GDC column names
  candidates <- c("project_id", "project", "projectId", "cases.project.project_id")
  col <- candidates[candidates %in% colnames(meta)][1]
  if (is.na(col)) {
    stop("[ERROR] No project column found in colData. Available columns: ", 
         paste(colnames(meta), collapse = ", "))
  }
  
  proj <- meta[[col]]
  # Ensure rownames match sample IDs
  if (is.null(rownames(meta))) {
    rownames(meta) <- sample_ids[seq_len(nrow(meta))]
  }
  names(proj) <- rownames(meta)
  
  # Align to expression sample IDs
  proj[sample_ids]
}

## Helper: Filter samples by tumour purity threshold -------------------------

Added sample retention plotting to both TCGA scripts. Summary of changes:

## Changes made to both `TCGA_BRCA.R` and `TCGA_LAML.R`:

### 1) Added helper function `get_project_from_meta()`
- Extracts project information from SummarizedExperiment `colData()`
- Tries common TCGA/GDC column names: `project_id`, `project`, `projectId`, `cases.project.project_id`
- Aligns project labels with sample IDs

### 2) Added retention tracking
- Before filtering: records `n_before` and `project_before_tab` (project counts)
- After filtering: records `n_after` and `project_after_tab` (project counts)
- Logs retention message: `[RETENTION] TCGA-BRCA/LAML: X -> Y (Z% kept)`

### 3) Added sample retention bar plot
- RBL-style bar plot showing before/after counts by project
- Before filtering bars: grey (`#D3D3D3`)
- After filtering bars: Set2 colors (one per project)
- Includes count labels on bars
- Title: `"TCGA-BRCA/LAML: Sample Retention by Project and Purity Filtering"`
- Saved as: `TCGA-BRCA/LAML_sample_retention_by_project_same_axis.pdf`

### 4) Moved `tag` definition earlier
- Defined at the start of the function so it can be used throughout

## Output files

Both scripts now generate:
1. Sample retention plot: `{project}_sample_retention_by_project_same_axis.pdf`
2. Purity diagnostics: `{project}_purity_diagnostics.pdf`
3. Scores CSV: `{project}_tidyestimate_scores.csv`
4. Updated/filtered RDS files

The retention plot matches the RBL style and shows how many samples are retained after purity filtering, grouped by project (useful if there are multiple projects or subtypes).

Perfect — then you should **not** do “by project” at all. Just make a **2-bar plot (Before/After)** per cohort and print the retention line.

Drop in this minimal block right after you create `tcga_se_filtered`:

```r
# --- Retention summary (cohort-level: Before -> After) ---
n_before <- ncol(tcga_se)
n_after  <- ncol(tcga_se_filtered)

message(sprintf(
  "[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d (%.1f%% kept)",
  tag, n_before, n_after, 100 * n_after / n_before
))

# --- Simple retention plot (2 bars: Before vs After) ---
df_ret <- data.frame(
  Stage = factor(c("Before", "After"), levels = c("Before", "After")),
  Count = c(n_before, n_after)
)

p_ret <- ggplot(df_ret, aes(x = Stage, y = Count, fill = Stage)) +
  geom_col(width = 0.65, color = "white", linewidth = 0.6) +
  geom_text(aes(label = Count), vjust = -0.6, fontface = "bold", size = 5) +
  labs(
    title = paste0(tag, ": Sample retention after purity filtering"),
    subtitle = sprintf("Purity ≥ %.1f | %d → %d (%.1f%% kept)",
                       purity_threshold, n_before, n_after, 100*n_after/n_before),
    x = NULL,
    y = "Number of samples"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
  theme(legend.position = "none")

ret_pdf <- file.path(output_dir, sprintf("%s_retention_before_after.pdf", tag))
ggsave(ret_pdf, p_ret, width = 6.5, height = 5, dpi = 300)
message("[SUCCESS] Retention plot saved: ", ret_pdf)
```

Then just run twice:

```r
run_tidyestimate_purity_analysis(focus_project = "TCGA-BRCA", purity_threshold = 0.7)
run_tidyestimate_purity_analysis(focus_project = "TCGA-LAML", purity_threshold = 0.7)
```

That will give you exactly:

* **BRCA: N_before → N_after**
* **LAML: N_before → N_after**

…and two PDFs you can drop straight into slides.

  if (apply_purity_filter && "tumourPurity" %in% colnames(estimate_scores)) {
    tcga_se_filtered <- filter_samples_by_purity(tcga_se, purity_threshold)
    saveRDS(tcga_se_filtered, tcga_se_filtered_path)

  # Generate sample retention plot (before diagnostic plots)
  if (apply_purity_filter && "tumourPurity" %in% colnames(estimate_scores) && 
      exists("project_before_tab") && exists("project_after_tab")) {
    message("[INFO] Generating bar plot (sample retention)...")
    total_before <- sum(project_before_tab)
    total_after  <- sum(project_after_tab)
    
    proj_levels <- sort(unique(c(names(project_before_tab), names(project_after_tab))))
    project_before_tab <- project_before_tab[proj_levels]
    project_after_tab  <- project_after_tab[proj_levels]
    project_before_tab[is.na(project_before_tab)] <- 0
    project_after_tab[is.na(project_after_tab)]   <- 0
    
    df_plot <- data.frame(
      Project = rep(proj_levels, 2),
      Count   = c(as.numeric(project_before_tab), as.numeric(project_after_tab)),
      Stage   = rep(c("Before Filtering", "After Filtering"), each = length(proj_levels))
    ) %>%
      dplyr::mutate(
        Project = factor(Project, levels = proj_levels),
        Stage   = factor(Stage, levels = c("Before Filtering", "After Filtering"))
      )
    
    n_proj <- length(proj_levels)
    palette <- RColorBrewer::brewer.pal(max(3, min(8, n_proj)), "Set2")[seq_len(n_proj)]
    project_colors <- setNames(palette, proj_levels)
    
    fill_vals <- c(
      setNames(rep("#D3D3D3", n_proj),
               paste("Before Filtering", proj_levels, sep = ".")),
      setNames(project_colors,
               paste("After Filtering", proj_levels, sep = "."))
    )
    
    p_bar <- ggplot(df_plot, aes(x = Project, y = Count,
                                 fill = interaction(Stage, Project))) +
      geom_col(position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
               width = 0.7, color = "white", linewidth = 0.6) +
      geom_text(
        aes(label = Count, group = Stage),
        position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
        vjust    = -0.6,
        size     = 4.2,
        fontface = "bold",
        color    = "black"
      ) +
      scale_fill_manual(values = fill_vals, name = NULL) +
      labs(
        title    = paste0(tag, ": Sample Retention by Project and Purity Filtering"),
        subtitle = sprintf("Purity >= %.1f | n = %d -> %d",
                           purity_threshold, total_before, total_after),
        x = "Project",
        y = "Number of Samples"
      ) +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
      theme(
        plot.title        = element_text(face = "bold", size = 16, hjust = 0.5),
        plot.subtitle     = element_text(size = 12, hjust = 0.5, color = "gray40"),
        axis.title.x      = element_text(face = "bold"),
        axis.title.y      = element_text(face = "bold"),
        legend.position   = "top",
        legend.title      = element_blank(),
        legend.text       = element_text(size = 11),
        panel.grid.major.x = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor   = element_blank(),
        panel.border       = element_rect(color = "gray80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )
    
    bar_pdf <- file.path(output_dir, sprintf("%s_sample_retention_by_project_same_axis.pdf", tag))
    ggsave(bar_pdf, p_bar, width = 10, height = 7, dpi = 300)
    message("[SUCCESS] Bar plot saved: ", bar_pdf)
  }

    # Record retention counts BEFORE filtering

                    nrow(filtered_counts), ncol(filtered_counts)))
    
    # Record retention counts AFTER filtering
    n_after <- ncol(tcga_se_filtered)
    
    message(sprintf(
      "[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d (%.1f%% kept)",
      tag, n_before, n_after, 100 * n_after / n_before
    ))
    
    # Simple retention plot (2 bars: Before vs After)
    df_ret <- data.frame(
      Stage = factor(c("Before", "After"), levels = c("Before", "After")),
      Count = c(n_before, n_after)
    )
    
    p_ret <- ggplot(df_ret, aes(x = Stage, y = Count, fill = Stage)) +
      geom_col(width = 0.65, color = "white", linewidth = 0.6) +
      geom_text(aes(label = Count), vjust = -0.6, fontface = "bold", size = 5) +
      labs(
        title = paste0(tag, ": Sample retention after purity filtering"),
        subtitle = sprintf("Purity ≥ %.1f | %d → %d (%.1f%% kept)",
                           purity_threshold, n_before, n_after, 100 * n_after / n_before),
        x = NULL,
        y = "Number of samples"
      ) +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
      theme(legend.position = "none")
    
    ret_pdf <- file.path(output_dir, sprintf("%s_retention_before_after.pdf", tag))
    ggsave(ret_pdf, p_ret, width = 6.5, height = 5, dpi = 300)
    message("[SUCCESS] Retention plot saved: ", ret_pdf)

    result$filtered_counts <- filtered_counts
    result$filtered_output_path <- tcga_se_filtered_path
  }

Simplified the retention plotting in both scripts. Summary of changes:

## Changes made to both `TCGA_BRCA.R` and `TCGA_LAML.R`:

### 1) Removed project-based tracking
- Removed `projects_before`, `project_before_tab`, `projects_after`, `project_after_tab`
- Removed the complex project-based bar plot

### 2) Added simple cohort-level retention tracking
- Records `n_before` (before filtering) and `n_after` (after filtering)
- Logs retention message: `[RETENTION] TCGA-BRCA/LAML: X -> Y (Z% kept)`

### 3) Added simple 2-bar retention plot
- Two bars: "Before" and "After"
- Count labels on bars
- Title: `"{project}: Sample retention after purity filtering"`
- Subtitle shows purity threshold and retention percentage
- Saved as: `{project}_retention_before_after.pdf` (6.5" × 5")

## Output files

Both scripts now generate:
1. Simple retention plot: `TCGA-BRCA/LAML_retention_before_after.pdf`
2. Purity diagnostics: `TCGA-BRCA/LAML_purity_diagnostics.pdf`
3. Scores CSV: `TCGA-BRCA/LAML_tidyestimate_scores.csv`
4. Updated/filtered RDS files

The retention plot shows cohort-level before/after counts, suitable for slides. The `get_project_from_meta()` helper function remains but is unused; it can be removed later if needed.

user:
<user_query>
@\home\chu25\TCGA\all_tcga_count_data\tumour_purity\TCGA_LAML.R 

Nice — your **LAML script is basically correct** for the goal “LAML: N_before → N_after” and you already fixed the diagnostic titles to use `tag`. ✅

There’s only **one critical thing** I’d change (same as in BRCA), otherwise `filter_common_genes()` can be wrong / fragile:

## ✅ Critical fix: give `filter_common_genes()` an `hgnc_symbol` column

Right now you pass:

```r
as.data.frame(expr_hgnc), id = "hgnc_symbol"
```

…but `expr_hgnc` has gene symbols in **rownames**, not in a column called `hgnc_symbol`.

Replace your “Filter to ESTIMATE signature genes…” block with this:

```r
message("[INFO] Filtering to ESTIMATE signature genes...")

expr_hgnc_df <- as.data.frame(expr_hgnc) %>%
  tibble::rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")

expr_sig <- suppressMessages(
  tidyestimate::filter_common_genes(
    expr_hgnc_df,
    id = "hgnc_symbol",
    tidy = FALSE,
    tell_missing = TRUE,
    find_alias = TRUE
  )
)
```

That’s the main change.

---

## Small polish (optional but worth it)

### 1) Don’t keep `get_project_from_meta()` in this script

You’re not using it anymore (since your retention is cohort-level). You can delete it to reduce clutter.

### 2) Guard against “purity missing” more gracefully

Right now purity filtering only happens if `"tumourPurity" %in% colnames(estimate_scores)`. That’s OK, but if anything goes wrong with score column naming, you’ll skip retention entirely.

More robust pattern:

* always compute `n_before`
* attempt to add purity
* attempt to filter (your filter function already skips if purity missing)
* compute `n_after` regardless

But this is optional since your pipeline is working.

---

  old_string: ## Helper: Get project from SummarizedExperiment colData ---------------------
get_project_from_meta <- function(meta, sample_ids) {
  # Try common TCGA/GDC column names
  candidates <- c("project_id", "project", "projectId", "cases.project.project_id")
  col <- candidates[candidates %in% colnames(meta)][1]
  if (is.na(col)) {
    stop("[ERROR] No project column found in colData. Available columns: ", 
         paste(colnames(meta), collapse = ", "))
  }
  
  proj <- meta[[col]]
  # Ensure rownames match sample IDs
  if (is.null(rownames(meta))) {
    rownames(meta) <- sample_ids[seq_len(nrow(meta))]
  }
  names(proj) <- rownames(meta)
  
  # Align to expression sample IDs
  proj[sample_ids]
}

get_project_from_meta <- function(meta, sample_ids) {
  # Try common TCGA/GDC column names
  candidates <- c("project_id", "project", "projectId", "cases.project.project_id")
  col <- candidates[candidates %in% colnames(meta)][1]
  if (is.na(col)) {
    stop("[ERROR] No project column found in colData. Available columns: ", 
         paste(colnames(meta), collapse = ", "))
  }
  
  proj <- meta[[col]]
  # Ensure rownames match sample IDs
  if (is.null(rownames(meta))) {
    rownames(meta) <- sample_ids[seq_len(nrow(meta))]
  }
  names(proj) <- rownames(meta)
  
  # Align to expression sample IDs
  proj[sample_ids]
}

user:
<user_query>
That error is coming from **`filter_common_genes()`** (it uses tidyverse helpers internally) and it means your input data frame has **duplicate column names**.

In your case, this is almost certainly because you did:

```r
expr_hgnc_df <- as.data.frame(expr_hgnc) %>% rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")
```

but **your expression columns (sample IDs) are not unique** (duplicate TCGA barcodes happen a lot: multiple aliquots, portions, or repeated entries), so `as.data.frame(expr_hgnc)` already has duplicated column names → tidyverse refuses it.

### Fix: enforce unique sample IDs *before* running ESTIMATE

Do this right after you extract the expression matrix (before mapping):

```r
expr_matrix <- assay(tcga_se)

# --- Ensure unique sample IDs (critical) ---
if (anyDuplicated(colnames(expr_matrix)) > 0) {
  dup <- colnames(expr_matrix)[duplicated(colnames(expr_matrix))]
  message(sprintf("[WARN] Found %d duplicated sample IDs. Making them unique.", length(unique(dup))))
  
  # Make unique names by appending _dup1, _dup2, ...
  colnames(expr_matrix) <- make.unique(colnames(expr_matrix), sep = "_dup")
}
```

Then proceed as normal.

This is the fastest way to get your pipeline running.

---

## Better (recommended) fix: collapse duplicates instead of renaming

Renaming keeps both duplicates, which might be okay for purity scoring, but biologically you might prefer 1 sample per patient/sample ID.

A robust “collapse duplicates by summing counts” (genes × samples):

```r
collapse_duplicate_samples <- function(mat) {
  stopifnot(!is.null(colnames(mat)))
  if (anyDuplicated(colnames(mat)) == 0) return(mat)

  message("[INFO] Collapsing duplicated sample IDs by summing columns...")
  df <- as.data.frame(mat)
  df$gene_id <- rownames(mat)

  df_long <- tidyr::pivot_longer(df, -gene_id, names_to="sample", values_to="count")
  df_sum  <- df_long %>%
    dplyr::group_by(gene_id, sample) %>%
    dplyr::summarise(count = sum(count), .groups="drop") %>%
    tidyr::pivot_wider(names_from = sample, values_from = count)

  out <- as.matrix(df_sum[,-1])
  rownames(out) <- df_sum$gene_id
  storage.mode(out) <- "numeric"
  out
}
```

Call it right after you extract `expr_matrix`:

```r
expr_matrix <- collapse_duplicate_samples(expr_matrix)
```

*(This uses tidyr; you already load it indirectly sometimes, but if not, add `library(tidyr)`.)*

---

## Quick diagnostic to confirm duplicates

Before fixing, you can print:

```r
message("[INFO] Any duplicated sample IDs? ", anyDuplicated(colnames(expr_matrix)))
```

### What I’d do for your thesis pipeline

* **Collapse duplicates** (preferred)
* Then run purity + retention

Because otherwise your “N_before” count is inflated by repeated barcodes and your retention plot becomes less interpretable.

```r
anyDuplicated(colnames(assay(tcga_se)))

```

I can tell you whether these are exact duplicates or need truncation to patient-level barcodes (e.g., first 12 chars of TCGA barcode).

  if (anyDuplicated(colnames(mat)) == 0) return(mat)

Added duplicate sample ID handling to both scripts. Summary:

## Changes made to both `TCGA_BRCA.R` and `TCGA_LAML.R`:

### 1) Added `tidyr` library
- Added `library(tidyr)` to the library block (needed for `pivot_longer` and `pivot_wider`)

### 2) Added `collapse_duplicate_samples()` helper function
- Checks for duplicate column names (sample IDs)
- If duplicates exist, collapses them by summing counts per gene
- Uses `tidyr::pivot_longer()` → `group_by()` → `summarise()` → `pivot_wider()` to sum duplicate samples
- Returns a matrix with unique sample IDs

### 3) Added duplicate detection and handling
- Right after extracting `expr_matrix`, checks for duplicates using `anyDuplicated()`
- If duplicates found:
  - Logs a warning with count of unique duplicate IDs
  - Calls `collapse_duplicate_samples()` to sum counts
  - Logs the new matrix dimensions
- If no duplicates:
  - Logs that all sample IDs are unique

## Benefits

- Prevents `filter_common_genes()` errors from duplicate column names
- More accurate retention counts (no inflated "N_before" from duplicates)
- Biologically appropriate: combines multiple aliquots/portions of the same sample
- Clear logging of when duplicates are found and collapsed

The scripts now handle duplicate TCGA barcodes by summing expression counts, which is appropriate for purity analysis.

@\home\chu25\TCGA\all_tcga_count_data\tumour_purity\TCGA_BRCA.R 

Got it — since your sample IDs are **unique**, the duplicate-column problem is happening **inside the data.frame you pass to `tidyestimate::filter_common_genes()`**, not in the original matrix.

This is a super common gotcha because `as.data.frame()` / tibble conversion can still yield **duplicate column names** (e.g., due to name-repair steps or hidden duplicates introduced during conversion).

### ✅ Do this: force-unique column names right before `filter_common_genes()`

Insert this **immediately before** your `filter_common_genes()` call:

```r
message("[INFO] Filtering to ESTIMATE signature genes...")

expr_hgnc_df <- as.data.frame(expr_hgnc, check.names = FALSE) %>%
  tibble::rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")

# --- HARD GUARD: enforce unique column names ---
dups <- duplicated(colnames(expr_hgnc_df))
if (any(dups)) {
  message("[WARN] Duplicate column names detected in expr_hgnc_df. Fixing with make.unique().")
  message("[WARN] Duplicated names: ", paste(unique(colnames(expr_hgnc_df)[dups]), collapse = ", "))
  colnames(expr_hgnc_df) <- make.unique(colnames(expr_hgnc_df), sep = "_dup")
}

expr_sig <- suppressMessages(
  tidyestimate::filter_common_genes(
    expr_hgnc_df,
    id = "hgnc_symbol",
    tidy = FALSE,
    tell_missing = TRUE,
    find_alias = TRUE
  )
)
```

That should stop the crash immediately.

---

## Why this happens even when sample IDs are unique

Because the error refers to **columns in `x`** (the data.frame you pass), and those columns include:

* your sample columns **plus**
* the newly added `hgnc_symbol` column
* any name-repair artifacts from conversion

So the matrix can be fine, but the converted data.frame can still end up with duplicated names.

---

## Optional: add a one-line sanity check (so you see it in logs)

Right after you build `expr_hgnc_df`:

```r
message("[INFO] expr_hgnc_df duplicated colnames: ", any(duplicated(colnames(expr_hgnc_df))))
```

---

If you run again and it still errors, paste the output of the “Duplicated names:” line — then we’ll know exactly what name is colliding (and why).

</user_query>

@\home\chu25\TCGA\all_tcga_count_data\tumour_purity\TCGA_LAML.R 

In your **RBL script**, you never call the function that’s throwing the error.

### What’s failing in BRCA/LAML

The error comes from:

```r
tidyestimate::filter_common_genes(...)
```

That function internally does tidyr/dplyr/tibble operations that **require unique column names**, and it’s deciding they are “not unique” (even if *your* `colnames()` look fine). That’s why you keep seeing:

> `Input columns in x must be unique.`

This can happen for reasons that are not caught by a simple `duplicated(colnames(x))`, e.g.:

* a hidden name-repair step inside tidyverse
* duplicated names created after some internal reshape/selection
* special names like `...1`, `...2` getting regenerated
* tibble “repair” rules being stricter than base R checks

### How your RBL script *avoids* the problem

Your RBL pipeline does this instead:

```r
# Manual common-gene filter (avoid filter_common_genes bug)
common_symbols <- unique(tidyestimate::common_genes$hgnc_symbol)
keep_genes <- intersect(rownames(counts_hgnc), common_symbols)
counts_sig <- counts_hgnc[keep_genes, , drop = FALSE]
df_for_estimate <- as.data.frame(counts_sig) %>% rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")
scores_raw <- score_fn(df_for_estimate, is_affymetrix = FALSE)
```

So the key point is:

✅ **No `filter_common_genes()` call**
✅ You do the same biological step (“keep ESTIMATE genes”) purely with base indexing (`intersect()` + matrix subset).
✅ Then you hand tidyestimate a simple `data.frame` with `hgnc_symbol` + one column per sample.

That’s why the RBL script “handles it”: **it bypasses the buggy/strict function**.

---

## If you want BRCA/LAML to behave like RBL

Do the same thing there: remove `filter_common_genes()` and replace it with the manual block:

```r
common_symbols <- unique(tidyestimate::common_genes$hgnc_symbol)
keep_genes <- intersect(rownames(expr_hgnc), common_symbols)
expr_sig_mat <- expr_hgnc[keep_genes, , drop = FALSE]
expr_sig <- as.data.frame(expr_sig_mat, check.names = FALSE) %>%
  tibble::rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")
```

That’s the direct reason your RBL pipeline works while BRCA/LAML fails.

## Changes made to both `TCGA_BRCA.R` and `TCGA_LAML.R`:

### Replaced `filter_common_genes()` with manual filtering

**Removed:**
- `tidyestimate::filter_common_genes()` call (which was causing the duplicate column name error)
- All the duplicate column name guards (no longer needed)

**Added (RBL-style manual filtering):**
- `common_symbols <- unique(tidyestimate::common_genes$hgnc_symbol)` - Gets ESTIMATE signature genes

- Converts to data.frame with `hgnc_symbol` column for scoring

- Avoids `filter_common_genes()` bugs: no internal tidyverse operations that can fail on column names
- Simpler and more reliable: uses base R `intersect()` and matrix subsetting
- Matches RBL script behavior: same approach that works in RBL
- Clear logging: shows how many ESTIMATE signature genes were found

Here’s what **`hema_tumour_purity_estimation.R`** uses as input and how it was likely run for that file.

## Inputs defined in the script

The script is meant to be run from the command line with:

| Argument       | Meaning |
|----------------|--------|
| **`--expr`**   | Path to the **expression count matrix** RDS (genes × samples or samples × genes; orientation is auto-detected). |
| **`--map`**    | Path to the **Ensembl → HGNC mapping** TSV (columns `Ensembl_ID`, `HGNC_Symbol`). |
| **`--outdir`** | Directory where outputs (scores, filtered RDS, plots) are written. |
| **`--threshold`** | (Optional) Purity threshold; default `0.7`. |
| **`--meta`**   | (Optional) Sample metadata CSV (with `sample` and cohort info) for cohort plots. |

So the **inputs** for any run are: the file passed to `--expr` and the file passed to `--map` (plus optional `--meta`).

## How the Snakefile runs it (per project)

When you run the pipeline via Snakemake, the rule `project_tumour_purity` calls the script with:

- **`--expr`**: `{DATA_ROOT}/{project}/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds`  
  e.g. `/home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds`
- **`--map`**: `{DATA_ROOT}/{project}/results/aggregated/ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv`
- **`--outdir`**: `{DATA_ROOT}/{project}/results/aggregated/tumour_purity`
- **`--meta`**: `{DATA_ROOT}/{project}/results/aggregated/sample_metadata.csv`

So in that setup, the **input** to the script is the **per-project** `raw_count_matrix.rds` and `ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv` for that `project`.

## Your file under `hema_aggregated`

The file you asked about is:

`/home/chu25/data/hema_aggregated/tumour_purity_results/hema_filtered_count_matrix_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`

That path is **not** one of the Snakemake rule outputs (those go under `DATA_ROOT/{project}/results/aggregated/tumour_purity/`). So that RDS was almost certainly produced by a **manual or custom** run of the same script with:

- **`--outdir`** (or equivalent) set to  
  `/home/chu25/data/hema_aggregated/tumour_purity_results`
- **`--expr`** set to some **aggregated HEMA count matrix** RDS (e.g. a combined CLL/BL/MPN or other HEMA matrix)
- **`--map`** set to an Ensembl→HGNC TSV (e.g. a shared `ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv` for that aggregated data)

The script does not record the exact `--expr`/`--map` paths it was called with. To see what the **actual** input was for that run you’d need to check:

- the command you used (e.g. in shell history), or  
- any wrapper script / config that calls `hema_tumour_purity_estimation.R` with `--outdir .../hema_aggregated/tumour_purity_results`.

So: **conceptually**, the input to the script that wrote that file was whatever RDS was passed as `--expr` (and the TSV passed as `--map`). **In practice**, for the run that produced `hema_aggregated/.../hema_filtered_count_matrix_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`, the exact input paths are not stored in the repo; they’re in the way the script was invoked (history or wrapper).

Here’s how parquet vs RDS are used.

## In the Snakemake pipeline

- **Parquet** is only an intermediate:
  - `aggregate_project_counts` writes `final_count_matrix.parquet`.
  - Two rules then convert parquet → RDS:
    - `convert_project_parquet_to_rds` → `raw_count_matrix_nodim.rds`
    - `map_project_dimnames_to_rds` → **`raw_count_matrix.rds`**
- **Downstream steps use RDS, not parquet:**
  - Tumour purity: `hema_tumour_purity_estimation.R` is run with  
    `--expr .../raw_count_matrix.rds`  
    So purity runs on the **RDS**, not the parquet.
  - Purity outputs are RDS:  
    `filtered_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`, `filtered_hgnc_purity0.7.rds`.
- **Leu/lym DSMZ** uses the **RDS** from the aggregated HEMA purity run:  
  `hema_filtered_count_matrix_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`  
  (in `io.R`).

So in this design you do **not** use parquet downstream; you use the RDS (raw count RDS for purity, then the filtered RDS for leu_lym).

## What that means for you

- If you **did** run the full Snakemake pipeline for each project, then you would have used **RDS** for purity and downstream (and parquet only as the step before RDS).
- For **MPN** you don’t have `raw_count_matrix.rds` (or the parquet the Snakefile expects); you have `final_hema_*` files. So for MPN it’s likely either:
  - the parquet → RDS → purity chain wasn’t run, or
  - a different workflow produced the `final_hema_*` (and possibly the single `hema_filtered_count_matrix_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`) from parquet or another source.

So: in the pipeline as written, **downstream uses RDS, not parquet**. If in practice you only see parquet (or only `final_hema_*`) and no RDS for MPN, that would mean the RDS conversion and/or purity step wasn’t run for that project in this pipeline, not that you “only used parquet downstream.”
