## What happened (now we can say it concretely)

### 1) The “hema_aggregated” purity run was **not actually aggregated across CLL + MPN + BL**

Even though you *expected* an aggregated HEMA matrix, the **filtered** output you inspected contains **only 10 samples**, all mapping to **GSE237907 (CLL)**.

So the run that produced:

`/home/chu25/data/hema_aggregated/tumour_purity_results/hema_filtered_count_matrix_ensembl_purity0.7.rds`

must have been executed on an input expression matrix that effectively contained **only the 10 CLL samples** (or it subset to them before writing).

### 2) MPN + Primary BL were **not included at input time**, not “filtered out”

This is the key inference:

* If MPN (6) + BL (8) were *present in the input matrix* and then failed purity ≥ 0.7, you would typically still see evidence in:

  * the **ESTIMATE scores CSV** (it would list all input samples with purity values),
  * or at least a “Before → After” drop from 24 → 10.

But your mapping table says:

* “In aggregated input”: CLL 10, MPN 6, BL 8 (24 total)
* Yet the **purity-filtered RDS has only those 10 CLL**.

That pattern most often means the “input” you’re calling aggregated input is coming from metadata files, but the **actual `--expr` matrix used for that run** was already CLL-only (or was accidentally aligned/merged in a way that kept only the intersection with CLL).

### 3) The most likely technical failure mode: **sample-ID mismatch / intersection collapse**

Your samples in the purity RDS are named `SRR25381845`… style SRR run IDs.

If the pipeline tried to “aggregate” by merging datasets and did something like:

* keep only samples that appear in **all** component matrices / metadata tables (`intersect`)
* or align counts to metadata using an inner join / intersection on sample IDs

then a mismatch between naming schemes can silently drop projects.

Example of how this happens in practice:

* CLL matrix uses **SRR IDs**
* MPN/BL matrix columns use **GSM IDs** or custom IDs
* metadata uses a different column than the one the count matrix uses
  → an `intersect()` or join keeps only the set that matches (here: CLL).

So the aggregated run didn’t “filter out” MPN/BL by purity; it likely **never carried them through** because the identifiers didn’t match during aggregation/alignment.

Open the **scores file** that was written by that aggregated run:

`/home/chu25/data/hema_aggregated/tumour_purity_results/estimate_scores.csv` (or similarly named)

* If it has **only 10 rows**, then the input to ESTIMATE was already CLL-only.
* If it has **24 rows** but the filtered matrix has 10 columns, then MPN/BL were present but failed purity (less likely given your other outputs, but possible).

If you paste the first ~30 lines of that scores CSV (header + a few rows), I can tell you immediately which case it is.

```bash
    ln -sf final_hema_ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv
  fi

```

This does **not copy data**; it just gives Snakemake the filenames it expects.

## Option 2: bypass Snakemake completely and write `raw_count_matrix.rds` directly from parquet

If you just want the raw matrices fast and don’t care about intermediates:

### R script: parquet → raw_count_matrix.rds

Save as `parquet_to_raw_rds.R`:

```r
#!/usr/bin/env Rscript
suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(arrow)
})

args <- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
if (length(args) < 2) {
  stop("Usage: parquet_to_raw_rds.R <final_hema_count_matrix.parquet> <raw_count_matrix.rds>")
}
in_parquet <- args[1]
out_rds    <- args[2]

mat <- arrow::read_parquet(in_parquet)

# arrow returns tibble/data.frame-like; convert to matrix
mat <- as.data.frame(mat, check.names = FALSE)

# If the parquet stores gene IDs as first column, set rownames from it.
# (Common patterns: "gene_id", "Ensembl_ID", or unnamed first column)
first_col <- colnames(mat)[1]
if (grepl("gene", first_col, ignore.case = TRUE) || first_col %in% c("X", "", NA)) {
  rownames(mat) <- mat[[1]]
  mat[[1]] <- NULL
}

m <- as.matrix(mat)
storage.mode(m) <- "numeric"

saveRDS(m, out_rds)
cat("[OK] Wrote:", out_rds, " dims=", nrow(m), "x", ncol(m), "\n")
```

Run for all 3 projects:

```bash
Rscript parquet_to_raw_rds.R \
  /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/final_hema_count_matrix.parquet \
  /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds

Rscript parquet_to_raw_rds.R \
  /home/chu25/data/mpn/GSE228758/results/aggregated/final_hema_count_matrix.parquet \
  /home/chu25/data/mpn/GSE228758/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds

Rscript parquet_to_raw_rds.R \
  /home/chu25/data/pri_bl/GSE199413/results/aggregated/final_hema_count_matrix.parquet \
  /home/chu25/data/pri_bl/GSE199413/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds
```

Two key takeaways for what you do next:

## 1) Your `raw_count_matrix.rds` is **samples × genes**

So:

* **N_before** = `nrow(raw)` (not `ncol`)
* Genes are on **columns** (`ENSG...`), samples are on **rows** (`SRR...`)

That also explains why your TCGA scripts (which assume **genes × samples**) were getting messy.

---

## 2) For purity + ESTIMATE, convert to **genes × samples** before mapping/scoring

### Minimal robust pattern (works for CLL/MPN/BL and TCGA alike)

Add this helper once:

```r
ensure_genes_by_samples <- function(mat) {
  stopifnot(!is.null(rownames(mat)), !is.null(colnames(mat)))

  row_is_ensg <- any(grepl("^ENSG", rownames(mat)))
  col_is_ensg <- any(grepl("^ENSG", colnames(mat)))

  # Desired: genes on rows
  if (!row_is_ensg && col_is_ensg) {
    message("[INFO] Detected samples×genes; transposing to genes×samples.")
    mat <- t(mat)
  }
  mat
}
```

Then right after loading:

```r
expr_matrix <- readRDS(".../raw_count_matrix.rds")
expr_matrix <- ensure_genes_by_samples(expr_matrix)
message(sprintf("[INFO] Matrix dims now: %d genes × %d samples", nrow(expr_matrix), ncol(expr_matrix)))
```

Now your existing `map_ensembl_to_hgnc()` and everything downstream behaves as intended.

---

```r
raw  <- readRDS(".../raw_count_matrix.rds")
raw  <- ensure_genes_by_samples(raw)
N_before <- ncol(raw)

filt <- readRDS(".../tumour_purity/filtered_ensembl_purity0.7.rds")
filt <- ensure_genes_by_samples(filt)
N_after <- ncol(filt)

cat(sprintf("[RETENTION] %s: %d -> %d\n", "GSE237907", N_before, N_after))
```

**B) If you only have `estimate_scores.csv`:**

```r
scores <- read.csv(".../estimate_scores.csv", row.names = 1)
N_before <- nrow(scores)
N_after  <- sum(scores$tumourPurity >= 0.7, na.rm = TRUE)
```

---

## Why you kept seeing: “Input columns in `x` must be unique.”

With your new raw RDS, this error will happen **if** you pass a **genes×samples matrix directly** into `filter_common_genes()` *without first converting to a data.frame with `hgnc_symbol` as a dedicated column*, OR if you accidentally feed **samples×genes** into the function (then genes become “columns”, and any duplication/naming issues explode).

So the safe sequence is:

1. `ensure_genes_by_samples()`
2. map Ensembl→HGNC
3. `expr_hgnc_df <- as.data.frame(expr_hgnc, check.names=FALSE) |> rownames_to_column("hgnc_symbol")`
4. run ESTIMATE

---

If you paste **one** of the project purity scripts you want to run next (CLL/MPN/BL), I’ll rewrite the “load + orient + N_before/N_after” part into a drop-in block so you can run all three projects with the exact same logic.

</user_query>

user:
<user_query>
Yep — that’s the right fix, and (importantly) it fixes the **root cause** for HEMA:

* your `raw_count_matrix.rds` files are **samples × genes**
* `tidyestimate` + your mapping/scoring expects **genes × samples**
* `ensure_genes_by_samples()` forces a consistent orientation **before anything else touches the matrix**

So now you’ll get a clean:

* **N_before = ncol(counts_genes_x_samples)**
* **N_after = number of kept samples after purity filter**

## Replace your `p_ret <- ...` block with this

```r
# Simple retention plot (2 bars: Before vs After)
df_ret <- data.frame(
  Stage = factor(c("Before", "After"), levels = c("Before", "After")),
  Count = c(n_before, n_after)
)

# Match RBL/NBL styling
before_grey <- "#D3D3D3"
after_col   <- RColorBrewer::brewer.pal(3, "Set2")[2]  # stable "Set2" colour

p_ret <- ggplot(df_ret, aes(x = Stage, y = Count, fill = Stage)) +
  geom_col(width = 0.7, color = NA) +
  geom_text(aes(label = Count), vjust = -0.6, fontface = "bold", size = 5) +
  scale_fill_manual(values = c("Before" = before_grey, "After" = after_col), guide = "none") +
  labs(
    title = paste0(tag, ": Sample retention after purity filtering"),
    subtitle = sprintf("Purity ≥ %.1f | %d → %d (%.1f%% kept)",
                       purity_threshold, n_before, n_after, 100 * n_after / n_before),
    x = NULL,
    y = "Number of samples"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
  theme(
    legend.position  = "none",
    panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
    panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
  )
```

Yep — you can generate **both PDFs per cohort (CLL / BL / MPN)**, and save them as:

Below is a **drop-in replacement** for your current `make_hema_cohort_plots()` that does exactly that.

It will:

* loop over cohorts
* make **purity diagnostics per cohort** (Purity vs Stromal, Purity vs Immune, Purity histogram)
* make **retention barplot per cohort** (Before vs After **by project**, or by cohort if project not present)
* remove internal grids everywhere
* ensure filenames use the cohort name (no “hema”)

---

## 1) Add these libraries at the top (you need ggpubr + RColorBrewer)

In your `suppressPackageStartupMessages({ ... })` add:

```r
library(ggpubr)
library(RColorBrewer)
```

---

## 2) Replace `make_hema_cohort_plots()` with this version

```r
make_hema_cohort_plots <- function(meta_csv, scores_df, purity_threshold, outdir) {
  if (!file.exists(meta_csv)) {
    message("[WARN] Metadata file not found, skipping HEMA cohort plots: ", meta_csv)
    return(invisible(NULL))
  }

  message("[INFO] Generating cohort-level plots from: ", meta_csv)

  meta <- read.csv(meta_csv, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
  if (!("sample" %in% colnames(meta))) stop("[ERROR] Metadata needs a 'sample' column.")

  # Derive cohort (CLL / BL / MPN)
  if ("cohort" %in% colnames(meta)) {
    meta$Cohort <- meta$cohort
  } else if ("Cohort" %in% colnames(meta)) {
    meta$Cohort <- meta$Cohort
  } else if ("project" %in% colnames(meta)) {
    meta$Cohort <- dplyr::case_when(
      meta$project == "GSE237907" ~ "CLL",
      meta$project == "GSE199413" ~ "BL",
      meta$project == "GSE228758" ~ "MPN",
      TRUE                        ~ NA_character_
    )
  } else {
    stop("[ERROR] Could not infer cohort; add 'cohort' or 'project' to metadata.")
  }

  meta$Cohort <- factor(meta$Cohort, levels = c("CLL", "BL", "MPN"))
  meta <- meta[!is.na(meta$Cohort), , drop = FALSE]

  # Join scores + meta
  scores_df <- as.data.frame(scores_df)
  scores_df <- tibble::rownames_to_column(scores_df, "sample")

  merged <- dplyr::inner_join(scores_df, meta, by = "sample")

  # Basic sanity
  needed <- c("tumourPurity", "StromalScore", "ImmuneScore")
  missing <- setdiff(needed, colnames(merged))
  if (length(missing) > 0) {
    warning("[WARN] Missing columns in merged data: ", paste(missing, collapse = ", "),
            "\nPurity diagnostics will be partial.")
  }

  # Use project as x-axis grouping if available; otherwise fall back to Cohort
  has_project <- "project" %in% colnames(merged)
  group_var <- if (has_project) "project" else "Cohort"

  # Cohort loop
  cohort_levels <- levels(meta$Cohort)

  # Style helpers (match your RBL/NBL vibe)
  before_grey <- "#D3D3D3"
  after_blue  <- "#1E88E5"
  pal_groups  <- RColorBrewer::brewer.pal(3, "Set2")[c(2, 1)]

  for (coh in cohort_levels) {
    message("[INFO] --- Cohort: ", coh, " ---")

    meta_c <- meta[meta$Cohort == coh, , drop = FALSE]
    merged_c <- merged[merged$Cohort == coh, , drop = FALSE]

    if (nrow(meta_c) == 0) {
      message("[WARN] No metadata samples for cohort: ", coh, " (skipping).")
      next
    }
    if (!"tumourPurity" %in% colnames(merged_c)) {
      message("[WARN] tumourPurity missing for cohort: ", coh, " (skipping purity diagnostics).")
    }

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # A) Retention by project (same-axis) WITHIN THIS COHORT
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # BEFORE counts are based on meta (all samples), AFTER counts are purity-kept
    before_tab <- if (has_project && "project" %in% colnames(meta_c)) table(meta_c$project) else table(meta_c$Cohort)
    grp_levels <- names(before_tab)

    # AFTER (only samples in merged_c with valid purity and passing threshold)
    after_tab <- integer(0)
    if ("tumourPurity" %in% colnames(merged_c)) {
      kept <- !is.na(merged_c$tumourPurity) & merged_c$tumourPurity >= purity_threshold
      if (has_project && "project" %in% colnames(merged_c)) {
        after_tab <- table(merged_c$project[kept])
      } else {
        after_tab <- table(merged_c$Cohort[kept])
      }
    }

    # unify levels
    grp_levels <- sort(unique(c(names(before_tab), names(after_tab))))
    before_counts <- as.numeric(before_tab[grp_levels]); before_counts[is.na(before_counts)] <- 0
    after_counts  <- as.numeric(after_tab[grp_levels]);  after_counts[is.na(after_counts)]  <- 0

    df_ret <- data.frame(
      Group = rep(grp_levels, 2),
      Count = c(before_counts, after_counts),
      Stage = rep(c("Before", "After"), each = length(grp_levels))
    )
    df_ret$Group <- factor(df_ret$Group, levels = grp_levels)
    df_ret$Stage <- factor(df_ret$Stage, levels = c("Before", "After"))

    total_before <- sum(before_counts)
    total_after  <- sum(after_counts)

    p_ret <- ggplot(df_ret, aes(x = Group, y = Count, fill = Stage)) +
      geom_col(position = position_dodge(width = 0.8), width = 0.7, colour = NA) +
      geom_text(
        aes(label = Count, group = Stage),
        position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
        vjust = -0.4, size = 3.8
      ) +
      scale_fill_manual(values = c("Before" = before_grey, "After" = after_blue), name = NULL) +
      labs(
        title    = sprintf("%s: Sample retention by %s", coh, if (has_project) "project" else "cohort"),
        subtitle = sprintf("Purity ≥ %.1f | n = %d → %d", purity_threshold, total_before, total_after),
        x        = if (has_project) "Project" else "Cohort",
        y        = "Number of samples"
      ) +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
      theme(
        plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
        plot.subtitle    = element_text(hjust = 0.5, colour = "grey40"),
        axis.title.x     = element_text(face = "bold"),
        axis.title.y     = element_text(face = "bold"),
        legend.position  = "top",
        panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
        panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )

    ret_pdf <- file.path(outdir, sprintf("%s_sample_retention_by_project_same_axis.pdf", coh))
    ggsave(ret_pdf, p_ret, width = 7.5, height = 5.5, dpi = 300)
    message("[INFO] Saved: ", ret_pdf)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    # B) Purity diagnostics (per cohort): Stromal/Immune correlations + histogram
    # ------------------------------------------------------------
    if (!all(c("tumourPurity", "StromalScore", "ImmuneScore") %in% colnames(merged_c))) {
      message("[WARN] Missing tumourPurity/StromalScore/ImmuneScore for cohort ", coh, "; skipping purity diagnostics.")
      next
    }

    plot_data <- merged_c %>%
      dplyr::filter(!is.na(tumourPurity)) %>%
      dplyr::mutate(
        PurityGroup = cut(
          tumourPurity,
          breaks = c(-Inf, purity_threshold, Inf),
          labels = c(paste0("<", purity_threshold), paste0(">=", purity_threshold)),
          include.lowest = TRUE
        )
      )

    if (nrow(plot_data) == 0) {
      message("[WARN] No non-NA tumourPurity values for cohort ", coh, "; skipping purity diagnostics.")
      next
    }

    p1 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = StromalScore, y = tumourPurity, colour = PurityGroup)) +
      geom_point(size = 2.2, alpha = 0.85, shape = 16) +
      geom_smooth(method = "lm", se = FALSE, colour = "grey30", linewidth = 0.8) +
      scale_colour_manual(values = pal_groups, name = "Purity") +
      ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "spearman", label.y.npc = "top", label.x.npc = "left", size = 4, colour = "black") +
      ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "pearson",  label.y.npc = "top", label.x.npc = "left", size = 4, colour = "black", vjust = 2) +
      labs(title = sprintf("%s: Purity vs Stromal score", coh),
           x = "Stromal Score", y = "Tumour Purity") +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
      theme(
        plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
        legend.position  = "top",
        panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
        panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )

    p2 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = ImmuneScore, y = tumourPurity, colour = PurityGroup)) +
      geom_point(size = 2.2, alpha = 0.85, shape = 16) +
      geom_smooth(method = "lm", se = FALSE, colour = "grey30", linewidth = 0.8) +
      scale_colour_manual(values = pal_groups, name = "Purity") +
      ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "spearman", label.y.npc = "top", label.x.npc = "left", size = 4, colour = "black") +
      ggpubr::stat_cor(method = "pearson",  label.y.npc = "top", label.x.npc = "left", size = 4, colour = "black", vjust = 2) +
      labs(title = sprintf("%s: Purity vs Immune score", coh),
           x = "Immune Score", y = "Tumour Purity") +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
      theme(
        plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
        legend.position  = "none",
        panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
        panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )

    p3 <- ggplot(plot_data, aes(x = tumourPurity)) +
      geom_histogram(bins = 30, alpha = 0.85, fill = pal_groups[2], colour = NA) +
      geom_vline(xintercept = purity_threshold, linetype = "dashed", colour = "firebrick", linewidth = 1) +
      labs(title = sprintf("%s: Tumour purity distribution", coh),
           x = "Tumour Purity", y = "Number of samples") +
      theme_minimal(base_size = 13) +
      theme(
        plot.title       = element_text(face = "bold", hjust = 0.5),
        panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
        panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
        panel.border     = element_rect(colour = "grey80", fill = NA, linewidth = 0.5)
      )

    combined <- ggpubr::ggarrange(
      p1, p2, p3,
      ncol = 2, nrow = 2,
      labels = c("A", "B", "C"),
      font.label = list(size = 14, face = "bold")
    )

    diag_pdf <- file.path(outdir, sprintf("%s_purity_diagnostics.pdf", coh))
    ggsave(diag_pdf, combined, width = 14, height = 10, dpi = 300)
    message("[INFO] Saved: ", diag_pdf)
  }

  invisible(NULL)
}
```

## How it's currently run

### Option 1: Via Snakemake (automatic)
The Snakefile rule `project_tumour_purity` calls the R script. When you run:
```bash
snakemake .../tumour_purity/filtered_ensembl_purity0.7.rds
```
Snakemake executes:
```bash
Rscript scripts/hema_tumour_purity_estimation.R \
  --expr {input.expr_rds} \
  --map {input.map_tsv} \
  --outdir {params.outdir} \
  --threshold {params.threshold} \
  --meta {input.meta_csv}
```

### Option 2: Direct Rscript call (manual)
You can run it directly with Rscript, as shown earlier:
```bash
Rscript /home/chu25/TCGA/hema_pipeline/hema_pipeline/scripts/hema_tumour_purity_estimation.R \
  --expr   /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/raw_count_matrix.rds \
  --map    /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/ensembl_to_hgnc.tsv \
  --outdir /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/tumour_purity \
  --threshold 0.7 \
  --meta   /home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/sample_metadata.csv
```

Should I create one? If so, specify:

- SLURM submission script, or a simple bash wrapper?

<mcp_instructions description="Instructions provided by MCP servers to help use them properly">
Server: cursor-browser-extension
The cursor-browser-extension is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Please use this server if the user is asking about questions or tasks related to frontend / webapp development, and you are encouraged to test any of your code changes by using the tools from this MCP server.

Server: cursor-ide-browser
The cursor-ide-browser is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Use this for frontend/webapp development and testing code changes.

CRITICAL - Lock/unlock workflow:
1. browser_lock requires an existing browser tab - you CANNOT lock before browser_navigate
2. Correct order: browser_navigate -> browser_lock -> (interactions) -> browser_unlock
3. If a browser tab already exists (check with browser_tabs list), call browser_lock FIRST before any interactions
4. Only call browser_unlock when completely done with ALL browser operations for this turn

IMPORTANT - Before interacting with any page:
1. Use browser_tabs with action "list" to see open tabs and their URLs
2. Use browser_snapshot to get the page structure and element refs before any interaction (click, type, hover, etc.)

IMPORTANT - Waiting strategy:
When waiting for page changes (navigation, content loading, animations, etc.), prefer short incremental waits (1-3 seconds) with browser_snapshot checks in between rather than a single long wait. For example, instead of waiting 10 seconds, do: wait 2s -> snapshot -> check if ready -> if not, wait 2s more -> snapshot again. This allows you to proceed as soon as the page is ready rather than always waiting the maximum time.

Notes:
- Native dialogs (alert/confirm/prompt) never block automation. By default, confirm() returns true and prompt() returns the default value. To test different responses, call browser_handle_dialog BEFORE the triggering action: use accept: false for "Cancel", or promptText: "value" for custom prompt input.
- Iframe content is not accessible - only elements outside iframes can be interacted with.
- Use browser_type to append text, browser_fill to clear and replace. browser_fill also works on contenteditable elements.
- For nested scroll containers, use browser_scroll with scrollIntoView: true before clicking elements that may be obscured.
</mcp_instructions>

The script performs:
1. Loads TCGA-DLBC expression data
2. Maps Ensembl → HGNC gene symbols
3. Runs ESTIMATE scoring (tumour purity estimation)
4. Filters samples by purity threshold (default 0.50)
5. Generates retention barplots and purity diagnostic plots

user:
<user_query>
@\home\chu25\TCGA\tcga_dlbc\tumour_purity_estimation.R 

To remove the **internal gridlines** from *all* plots in your TCGA-DLBC script, add **one theme helper** and apply it to `p_bar`, `p1`, `p2`, `p3` (or set it globally).

## Option A (recommended): define once, reuse everywhere

Add this near the top (after libraries):

```r
theme_no_grid <- function() {
  theme(
    panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank()
  )
}
```

Then update each plot by adding `+ theme_no_grid()` at the end.

### 1) Bar plot (`p_bar`)

Change:

```r
p_bar <- ggplot(...) +
  ... +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
  theme(
    ...
    panel.grid.major.x = element_blank(),
    panel.grid.minor   = element_blank(),
    ...
  )
```

to:

```r
p_bar <- ggplot(df_plot, aes(x = Project, y = Count, fill = interaction(Stage, Project))) +
  geom_col(position = position_dodge(width = 0.8), width = 0.7, color = "white", size = 0.6) +
  geom_text(
    aes(label = Count, group = Stage),
    position = position_dodge(width = 0.8),
    vjust = -0.6, size = 4.2, fontface = "bold", color = "black"
  ) +
  scale_fill_manual(values = fill_vals, name = NULL) +
  labs(
    title    = "TCGA-DLBC: Sample Retention by Purity Filtering",
    subtitle = sprintf("Purity ≥ %.1f | n = %d → %d", purity_threshold, total_before, total_after),
    x = "Project", y = "Number of Samples"
  ) +
  theme_minimal(base_size = 14) +
  theme_no_grid() +
  theme(
    plot.title     = element_text(face = "bold", size = 16, hjust = 0.5),
    plot.subtitle  = element_text(size = 12, hjust = 0.5, color = "gray40"),
    axis.title.x   = element_text(face = "bold"),
    axis.title.y   = element_text(face = "bold"),
    legend.position = "top",
    legend.title   = element_blank(),
    legend.text    = element_text(size = 11),
    panel.border   = element_rect(color = "gray80", fill = NA, size = 0.5)
  )
```

(Notice we removed the *x-only* grid removal and replaced with **both axes** via `theme_no_grid()`.)

# --------------------------------------------------------------
# Theme helper: remove all internal gridlines
# --------------------------------------------------------------
theme_no_grid <- function() {
  theme(
    panel.grid.major = element_blank(),
    panel.grid.minor = element_blank()
  )
}

<user_query>

@/home/chu25/dsmz/leu_lym_dsmz_analysis/preprocessing
From what you showed, **your SLURM job ran the bash wrapper**, but **your R script produced *zero* stdout/stderr**:

* `leu_lym_dsmz_io_%j.out` contains only the two `echo` lines from `io.sh`
* `leu_lym_dsmz_io_%j.err` is empty
* `squeue` shows nothing → the job already finished

That usually means one of these happened:

1. **`Rscript leu_lym_dsmz_main.R` never executed** (e.g., script path wrong, or `cd` wrong, or `Rscript` missing), *but you’d normally see an error in `.err`*
2. **`Rscript` executed but exited immediately** before printing anything (e.g., early `quit()` somewhere, or it died before your first `cat()` line)
3. **stdout buffering + crash before flush** (less common, but can happen if it crashes hard, e.g., native library mismatch)

The fastest way to locate the problem is to make the wrapper *prove* what happened.

---

## 1) Patch your `io.sh` to print diagnostics + capture R exit code

Replace the bottom part of `io.sh` with this (keep your SBATCH header as-is):

```bash
set -euo pipefail

source ~/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh
conda activate tcga-r-env || { echo "Failed to activate 'tcga-r-env' env"; exit 1; }

echo "[INFO] Conda environment activated"
export R_LIBS_USER=""

echo "[DEBUG] whoami=$(whoami)"
echo "[DEBUG] hostname=$(hostname)"
echo "[DEBUG] pwd_before_cd=$(pwd)"
echo "[DEBUG] which Rscript: $(which Rscript || true)"
Rscript --version || true

cd /home/chu25/dsmz/leu_lym_dsmz_analysis/preprocessing || { echo "Failed to change directory"; exit 1; }
echo "[DEBUG] pwd_after_cd=$(pwd)"
echo "[DEBUG] ls:"
ls -lah

echo "Starting Leukemia/Lymphoma DSMZ IO analysis..."
Rscript --vanilla leu_lym_dsmz_main.R
status=$?
echo "[DEBUG] Rscript exit code: $status"
exit $status
```

This will immediately tell you:

* whether `Rscript` exists in the env
* whether you’re in the directory you think you’re in
* whether `leu_lym_dsmz_main.R` is actually there
* the **exact exit code**

---

## 2) Add an “I definitely started” line at the *top* of your R script

At the very top of `leu_lym_dsmz_main.R` (before loading packages), add:

```r
cat(">>> leu_lym_dsmz_main.R started at ", as.character(Sys.time()), "\n", sep = "")
flush.console()
options(warn = 1)
```

If you don’t see that line in the `.out`, then **R never started or never reached the script**.

---

## 3) Most common pipeline-specific failure points in *your* script

These are the places I would suspect first (because they can fail early):

### A) `source(helper_scripts, chdir = TRUE)`

You’re sourcing:

```r
helper_scripts <- c("io.R", "dsmz_base_functions.R", ...)
invisible(lapply(helper_scripts, source, chdir = TRUE))
```

If those files are not in the current working directory **when the job runs**, `source()` will error and quit.

**Fix (robust):** source relative to the script location, not `getwd()`:

```r
script_dir <- normalizePath(dirname(sys.frame(1)$ofile %||% "leu_lym_dsmz_main.R"), mustWork = FALSE)
# Better in Rscript:
script_dir <- normalizePath(dirname(commandArgs(trailingOnly = FALSE)[grep("--file=", commandArgs(trailingOnly = FALSE))] |>
                                      sub("^--file=", "", x = _)), mustWork = TRUE)

helper_scripts <- file.path(script_dir, c(
  "io.R",
  "dsmz_base_functions.R",
  "tcga_base_functions.R",
  "tcga_dsmz_base_functions.R",
  "normalisation.R",
  "visualise.R",
  "helpers.R"
))
invisible(lapply(helper_scripts, function(f) { cat("[SOURCE] ", f, "\n"); source(f); }))
```

(If you don’t want to change structure: at least `cat(getwd()); list.files()` before sourcing.)

### B) `cfg = config` but `config` is not defined in this file

You call functions like:

```r
load_all_data <- function(cfg = config) { ... }
run_integrated_pipeline <- function(cfg = config) { ... }
```

If no sourced script defines `config`, you’ll crash when you call `run_integrated_pipeline()`.
Right now you don’t call it in this file (because the example usage is inside `if (FALSE)`), so **nothing runs** unless something in sourced scripts runs automatically.

So: **are you actually calling `run_integrated_pipeline()` anywhere?**
In the file you pasted: **no**.

✅ **If your intention is for the pipeline to run when you execute the script**, add at the bottom (outside `if (FALSE)`):

```r
results <- run_integrated_pipeline()
```

and make sure `config` exists (either defined in this file or sourced).

---

## 4) One-line “did anything run?” check (no code edits)

Run inside the job (or interactive node):

```bash
cd /home/chu25/dsmz/leu_lym_dsmz_analysis/preprocessing
Rscript --vanilla -e 'cat("PWD=",getwd(),"\n"); cat("FILES=",paste(list.files(),collapse=" "),"\n")'
```

If `leu_lym_dsmz_main.R` is there, then:

```bash
Rscript --vanilla leu_lym_dsmz_main.R; echo $?
```

This will instantly show whether it exits, hangs, or errors.

Right now your script defines a lot of functions, but **doesn’t actually execute the pipeline** (the only call is inside `if (FALSE)`), so it’s totally possible the R script exits cleanly doing nothing — which matches your logs.

If you tell me: **Do you expect `leu_lym_dsmz_main.R` to run automatically when called by SLURM?**
If yes, I’ll show you the cleanest “main()” pattern for Rscript (parse config, run, save sessionInfo, and always write a log).

* `check_file_exists(file_path, "TCGA-LAML RDS")`
* `stop("[ERROR] TCGA-LAML RDS not found: ...")`

So the problem is simply: **the path exists on your login node, but not on the compute node environment / filesystem view**, or the path is mistyped / permissioned.

Notice your `getwd()` inside the job is:

`/mnt/beegfs/mnt/work/bioinfo/home/chu25/...`

That’s a big clue: compute nodes are using a different mount layout than what you tested interactively (`/home/chu25/...`).

### What’s happening

On the compute node, `/home/chu25/data/...` likely **does not exist** (or is not mounted), while the “real” path is under something like:

`/mnt/beegfs/mnt/work/bioinfo/home/chu25/data/...`

Your config currently points to `/home/chu25/data/...`, so `file.exists()` returns FALSE → stop.

## Fix option A (best): make your config paths mount-agnostic by resolving to the “real” path at runtime

In `io.R` (where `config` is defined), add a small helper that **rewrites `/home/chu25/...` to the compute-node mount prefix if needed**.

Add this near the top of `io.R`:

```r
resolve_path <- function(p) {
  if (file.exists(p)) return(p)

  # Common HPC remap: /home/<user> -> /mnt/beegfs/mnt/work/bioinfo/home/<user>
  p2 <- sub("^/home/", "/mnt/beegfs/mnt/work/bioinfo/home/", p)
  if (file.exists(p2)) return(p2)

  # If nothing works, return original (and let checks fail with clear message)
  p
}
```

Then when you build `config`, wrap the paths:

```r
config <- list(
  tcga_laml_rds = resolve_path("/home/chu25/data/tcga/ALL_TCGA_STAR_Counts_SummarisedExperiment_filtered_TCGA-LAML.rds"),
  cll_rds       = resolve_path("/home/chu25/data/cll/GSE237907/results/aggregated/tumour_purity/filtered_ensembl_purity0.7.rds"),

  outdir        = resolve_path("/home/chu25/dsmz/leu_lym_dsmz_analysis/results")
)
```

Now it will work both on login and compute nodes.

Great — you’ve fixed the path problem. Now the pipeline is finding the TCGA file, but `load_tcga_laml()` is rejecting the object type:

> `[ERROR] Unknown data format for TCGA-LAML`

That means `readRDS(cfg$tcga_laml_rds)` returns **neither**:

* a `SummarisedExperiment` **(note: you used British spelling here)**
* nor a matrix/data.frame

So it’s likely a **`RangedSummarizedExperiment` / `SummarizedExperiment`**, **a `DESeqDataSet`**, or even a **list** that contains the counts.

### Important: your class check is wrong

You wrote:

```r
if (inherits(data, "SummarisedExperiment")) {
```

But the Bioconductor class is spelled **`SummarizedExperiment`** (American “z”), and the package is `SummarizedExperiment`.

So even if the object *is* a SummarizedExperiment, your `inherits()` check will fail.

✅ **Fix that first**, then broaden the accepted types.

---

## Fix: make `load_tcga_laml()` accept the common container classes

Replace the “Handle both SummarisedExperiment and matrix formats” block with this:

```r
data <- readRDS(file_path)

# 1) SummarizedExperiment (correct spelling)
if (inherits(data, "SummarizedExperiment")) {
  counts <- SummarizedExperiment::assay(data)
  cat("  Format: SummarizedExperiment\n")

# 2) DESeq2 object
} else if (inherits(data, "DESeqDataSet")) {
  counts <- as.matrix(SummarizedExperiment::assay(data))
  cat("  Format: DESeqDataSet\n")

# 3) matrix/data.frame
} else if (is.matrix(data) || is.data.frame(data)) {
  counts <- as.matrix(data)
  cat("  Format: Matrix\n")

# 4) list wrapper (common in saved pipelines)
} else if (is.list(data)) {
  # try common keys
  candidates <- c("counts", "count", "count_matrix", "expr", "assay", "mat")
  hit <- candidates[candidates %in% names(data)][1]
  if (!is.na(hit)) {
    counts <- data[[hit]]
    if (inherits(counts, "SummarizedExperiment")) {
      counts <- SummarizedExperiment::assay(counts)
      cat(sprintf("  Format: list -> %s (SummarizedExperiment)\n", hit))
    } else {
      counts <- as.matrix(counts)
      cat(sprintf("  Format: list -> %s\n", hit))
    }
  } else {
    stop(sprintf(
      "[ERROR] Unknown list structure for TCGA-LAML. Names: %s",
      paste(names(data), collapse = ", ")
    ))
  }

} else {
  stop(sprintf(
    "[ERROR] Unknown data format for TCGA-LAML. class(data) = %s",
    paste(class(data), collapse = ", ")
  ))
}
```

Also update your message lines to say “SummarizedExperiment” consistently.

user:
<user_query>
Perfect — TCGA is **Ensembl with version suffix** (`ENSG... .15`). So the “44 common genes” is almost certainly because **DSMZ rownames are Ensembl too, but without versions (or with different versions),** and you’re intersecting *strings*, so almost nothing matches.

### The fix is: **strip Ensembl versions everywhere before intersecting**, then collapse duplicates.

Do this in **io.R**, because that’s where the ID mismatch is introduced.

---

## 1) Add these helpers near the top of `io.R` (after `resolve_path()`)

```r
strip_ensembl_version <- function(x) sub("\\..*$", "", x)

strip_and_collapse <- function(mat) {
  rn0 <- rownames(mat)
  rn1 <- strip_ensembl_version(rn0)
  if (!identical(rn0, rn1)) rownames(mat) <- rn1

  # Collapse duplicates created by stripping versions
  if (any(duplicated(rownames(mat)))) {
    mat <- rowsum(mat, group = rownames(mat))
  }
  mat
}
```

---

## 2) Apply it in `load_tcga_laml()` and `load_cll()`

 resolve_path <- function(p) {
   # If the path exists as-is, use it
   if (file.exists(p)) return(p)
@@
   # Fall back to original; existence will be checked later with a clear error
   p

+# Ensembl ID helpers
+# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

+strip_ensembl_version <- function(x) sub("\\..*$", "", x)
+
+strip_and_collapse <- function(mat) {
+  rn0 <- rownames(mat)
+  rn1 <- strip_ensembl_version(rn0)
+  if (!identical(rn0, rn1)) {
+    rownames(mat) <- rn1
+  }
+  if (any(duplicated(rownames(mat)))) {
+    mat <- rowsum(mat, group = rownames(mat))
+  }
+  mat

## Update `make_dataset_meta()` so HEM gets a proper label + plotting name

Add HEM detection:

```r
cancer_type = case_when(

  grepl("rbl",  id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "RBL",
```

@\home\chu25\colDataAnnData\scripts\pan_cancer_umap_main.R 

This looks **correctly integrated** for HEM ✅ (split RAW + split batch-corrected VST, and your regex rules will label them properly).

There are just a few **important fixes / hardening tweaks** I’d make before you run it on HPC, so you don’t get silent mislabelling or ggplot issues:

---

## 1) Fix the `sample_type` regex (currently HEM cell lines will be mislabelled as Tumour)

Right now you have:

```r
sample_type = case_when(
  grepl("dsmz|ll100|cellline", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "Cell Line",
  TRUE ~ "Tumour"
)
```

But your HEM cellline key is `hem_dsmz_counts_raw` and `hem_dsmz_vst_bc` ✅ (good), so it matches `dsmz`.

However: if you ever rename keys like `hem_ll100_counts_raw`, it would still match `ll100`, but right now you used `hem_dsmz_...`, so OK.

Still, best practice is to include **both** `dsmz` and `cellline` and **also** `cl`-style patterns.

Use this stronger version:

```r
sample_type = case_when(
  grepl("dsmz|ll100|cellline|cell_line|cl_", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "Cell Line",
  TRUE ~ "Tumour"
)
```

---

## 2) Your `make_dataset_meta()` HEM regex has a typo-ish pattern (`l aml`)

You wrote:

```r
grepl("hem|l aml|laml|cll|leu|lym", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "HEM"
```

`l aml` with a space won’t match most ids. You don’t need it. Keep it clean:

```r
grepl("hem|laml|cll|leu|lym", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "HEM"
```

(And since your ids start with `hem_...`, that alone is already enough.)

---

## 3) You defined `theme_umap_main()` but you never use it

You currently use `theme_classic()` inside both plots. If you want your consistent style, swap to:

* tumour-only plot: replace `theme_classic()` with `theme_umap_main()`
* integrated plot: replace `theme_classic()` with `theme_umap_main()`

If you *want* classic, you can delete `theme_umap_main()`.

---

## 4) You should guard against **gene rowname versioning mismatch**

You strip `.15` etc via `clean_ensg_rows()`. Good.

But if any dataset uses **gene symbols** or has non-ENSG rows, your `Reduce(intersect, ...)` will still “work” but might crash your biology (common genes becomes tiny). Add a sanity check after `common_genes`:

```r
if (length(common_genes) < 5000) {
  stop("Common gene set too small (", length(common_genes),
       "). Likely mismatched gene identifiers across datasets.")
}
```

---

## 5) Small but important: remove `trimws()` from `coords$sample_id`

You do:

```r
coords$sample_id <- trimws(rownames(X_use))
```

If your sample IDs contain underscores etc it’s fine, but trimming can hide upstream weirdness. I’d keep it strict:

```r
coords$sample_id <- rownames(X_use)
```

Same for `annot_use$sample`.

---

## 6) How many are tumours vs cell lines (HEM sanity check)

From your IO log, HEM is:

* **Tumours:** 77 (TCGA-LAML = 67, CLL = 10)
* **Cell lines:** 109 (LL-100)

So once you run this script, your RAW/BATCH tables should show:

* HEM Tumour = 77
* HEM Cell Line = 109

If HEM shows **0 cell lines** in the table, then your `sample_type` regex is the first thing to inspect.

---

## Minimal patch block (copy/paste)

Replace your `make_dataset_meta()` with:

```r
make_dataset_meta <- function(cfg) {
  tibble(
    id   = names(cfg),
    path = unname(cfg)
  ) %>%
    mutate(
      cancer_type = case_when(
        grepl("nbl",  id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "NBL",
        grepl("brca|bcc", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "BRCA",
        grepl("rbl",  id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "RBL",
        grepl("hem|laml|cll|leu|lym", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "HEM",
        TRUE ~ "OTHER"
      ),
      sample_type = case_when(
        grepl("dsmz|ll100|cellline|cell_line|cl_", id, ignore.case=TRUE) ~ "Cell Line",
        TRUE ~ "Tumour"
      )
    )
}
```

That error means your **wrapper (shell) script is passing `--method ...`**, but your **R script does not define `--method`** (it only has `--pipe_root`, `--outdir`, `--dist_metrics`, `--n_neighbors`, `--min_dist`, `--seed`, `--page`). So `optparse/getopt` rejects the unknown long flag.

You fix it in **one of two ways** (pick whichever matches what you actually want):

---

## Option A (recommended): Remove `--method` from the shell wrapper

In whatever script prints:

```
[INFO] Running method: expr
```

you likely have something like:

```bash
Rscript pan_cancer_umap_main.R --method expr ...
```

Delete the `--method expr` part (and any other `--method ...` calls), because your R script already runs both blocks:

* RAW (`RAW_LOG1P_CPM`)
* BATCH (`BATCH_CORR_VST`)

So you don’t need a “method loop” outside.

**Example wrapper call:**

```bash
Rscript pan_cancer_umap_main.R \
  --pipe_root /home/chu25/colDataAnnData \
  --outdir /home/chu25/colDataAnnData/results/pan_cancer_umap_main \
  --dist_metrics euclidean,cosine \
  --n_neighbors 10 \
  --min_dist 0.5 \
  --seed 42 \
  --page slide
```

---

## Option B: Add a `--method` option to the R script (if you truly want to run only one block)

If you want your wrapper to run “expr” vs “batch” separately, then add this CLI option:

### 1) Add to `option_list`:

```r
make_option("--method", type="character", default="both",
            help="Which block to run: raw, batch, both [default: %default]")
```

### 2) Use it near the end (replace your last run section):

```r
method <- tolower(opt$method)

all_summ <- list()

if (method %in% c("raw","both")) {
  all_summ[["RAW"]] <- run_umap_block(X_raw_sxg, raw_obj$annot, "RAW_LOG1P_CPM")
}

if (method %in% c("batch","both")) {
  all_summ[["BATCH"]] <- run_umap_block(X_batch_sxg, batch_obj$annot, "BATCH_CORR_VST")
}

if (length(all_summ) == 0) stop("Invalid --method: ", opt$method, " (use raw, batch, both)")
```

Then your wrapper can do `--method raw` / `--method batch`.

---

## Quick way to find the offending wrapper

From `/home/chu25/colDataAnnData`, run:

```bash
grep -R --line-number --fixed-string "--method" .
```

That will show exactly which `.sh` is passing it.

---

If you paste your wrapper script (the one that prints “Running method: expr”), I’ll rewrite it cleanly to match your final R CLI (and avoid these flag mismatches permanently).
